Freedom 250 · Crypto · Reference · Crypto overview

The money: what is the dollar on the chain, who owes it, and what backs it?

This page reads every crypto dollar the library tracks: 36 coins and tokens (17 of them payment stablecoins or candidates), each with its exact issuing company, legal route, reserves and who holds them, attestation, redemption terms, size, freeze power (33 can freeze) and yield; then the July card decks that first mapped issuers, DeFi dollars, company control and GENIUS events; the Crypto Hub's 54 cash-leg nodes, which separate a stablecoin from a bank deposit and from central-bank money; and the dated filings and central-bank digital money moves. Who owes the dollar is always a named legal entity, not a brand or a chain.

Start with a question

line-upWho issues each dollar stablecoin, and who holds its reserves?The library tracks 17 dollar coins that are, or want to be, payment stablecoins. Those with a cited size total about $271.6 billion, and USD₮ (USDT) and USDC are 94% of it.question pageWhere does stablecoin interest go if the law bans paying it?The GENIUS Act bars an issuer from paying holders interest just for holding the coin. 10 of the 17 issuers here say in their terms that holders get nothing, and 7 don't say.line-upWho can freeze, burn or block a stablecoin?For 18 of the 36 crypto dollars here, the issuer can freeze or block coins, usually for suspected crime or on a court or government order.line-upWhich crypto dollars are not payment stablecoins under the law?19 of the crypto dollars in the library fall outside the Act's definition of a payment stablecoin: tokenized money-market funds and bank deposits (the Act's definition leaves out deposits and…line-upWhat has each card network and payment company actually launched with stablecoins?Of 32 stablecoin products from card networks and payment companies, 11 are fully live and 10 are live on a limited basis. 5 are pilots and 2 are announced only.line-upWhen a shop "accepts stablecoins," what does the shop actually receive?Usually ordinary money. In 14 of 27 products the shop is paid in dollars or local currency in its bank account and never touches a coin. In 7 it can choose, and in 6 it receives stablecoins.gridHow far has each big bank gone, capability by capability, and where does the proof stop?16 institutions across 7 capabilities, all 112 cells printed in words: 81 cells are live or live-limited, and in 17 cells the master and the bank comparison read the status differently (both shown).line-upCompare the money used to settle payments30 systems and money instruments, side by side: 5 stablecoin, 10 commercial-bank deposit token, 3 central-bank unit, 1 off-ledger final settlement, 1 carries more than one kind, 10 kind of money not…gridCompare companies’ roles in crypto finance7 company groups compared across the same 7 seats of a crypto money business, with Ripple as the baseline.     USDC · USD CoinLive

    Checked through: 2026-10-19 · Entity: USDC, Circle · Also shown on: Freeze power, Where the interest goes, Issuer and reserves
    Peg
    USD
    Kind
    payment stablecoin candidate
    Issuer legal entity
    Circle Internet Financial, LLC (outside the European Economic Area); Circle Internet Financial Europe SAS (inside the EEA)
    Parent company
    Circle Internet Group, Inc. (publicly listed)
    Brand or distributor
    Circle; distributed widely by exchanges and wallets, notably Coinbase
    Issuer jurisdiction
    United States (Circle Internet Financial, LLC); France / EEA (Circle Internet Financial Europe SAS)
    Genius route
    unresolved
    Genius route basis
    Circle's stated plan is to move U.S. issuance to a New York trust company (now chartered by NYDFS), but the October 15, 2026 USDC Terms still name Circle Internet Financial, LLC, and a state issuer above $10 billion outstanding must move to federal oversight within 360 days unless waived (Sec. 4(d)).
    Charter or license
    Circle Internet Trust Company LLC (doing business as Circle New York Trust) received a New York limited-purpose trust charter from NYDFS, announced July 31, 2026. Separately, Circle National Trust (First National Digital Currency Bank, N.A.) got final OCC approval July 10, 2026 and its charter took effect July 24, 2026; it does custody and trust work and does not issue USDC. Neither entity is the USDC issuer under the current terms.
    Official ids
    Occ charter number: 25361
    Reserves
    • Composition: Mostly shares of the Circle Reserve Fund (USDXX), which can hold cash, short-dated U.S. Treasuries and overnight Treasury repurchase agreements; the rest is cash, mostly at a handful of the world's largest banks.
    • Custodians: BlackRock (manages the Circle Reserve Fund), BNY (primary reserve custodian per library July 2026 cards; not named on the current transparency page)
    • Latest attestation: By: A Big Four accounting firm (not named on Circle's page); Date: 2026-08
    Redemption
    Direct 1:1 redemption only for holders with a Circle Mint account who pass identity checks (in practice institutions); less fees in Circle's fee schedule. Everyone else sells or redeems through an exchange, wallet or other intermediary.
    Market size
    • Usd: 72,500,000,000
    • As of: 2026-10-15
    • Basis: USDC in circulation, rounded on Circle's page (Circle's Aug 31 attestation: 73,320,757,305)
    Live since
    2018-09-26
    Status
    live
    Chains
    Ethereum, Solana, Avalanche C-Chain, Stellar, Hedera, Algorand, XDC, Arc (used to pay network fees)
    Freeze control
    • Can freeze: Yes
    • Who: Circle (the issuer)
    • How: Circle may block addresses and freeze USDC, temporarily or permanently, that it believes is tied to illegal activity or terms violations, and acts on legal orders from government authorities (USDC Terms, Section 13).
    Library records
    • genius_stablecoin_cards.json#usdc
    • company_control_cards.json#circle
    • TOKENIZATION_ENTITY_RELATIONSHIP_REGISTRY#usdc,circle-us-issuer,circle-trust-bank
    • CORPORATE_STACK_COMPARISON#circle
    Evidence through
    2026-10-19
    Verified on web
    Yes
    Web checked
    issuer (USDC Terms 2026-10-15), freeze terms, reserve page, NYDFS charter, market size, Coinbase rewards
    Open questions
    • When, if ever, will USDC issuance move to Circle Internet Trust Company LLC, and will it use the state route with a Sec. 4(d) waiver or move to federal oversight?
    • BNY's reserve-custody role is in the July library cards but not on the current transparency page; confirm.
    • Full chain list not compiled; the list above is only chains named in the library.
    Yield
    • Holder paid interest: No
    • Where reserve income goes: To Circle. USDC Terms say USDC itself pays holders nothing and holders have no right to reserve income. Circle reported $652.5 million of reserve income in Q1 2026 against $41.6 million of other revenue, and pays a large share to distributors: $324.6 million to Coinbase in Q2 2026.
    • Rewards via third parties: Coinbase runs USDC Rewards, which it calls 'a loyalty program funded by Coinbase'; in the U.S. it requires a paid Coinbase One membership and the rate is set by Coinbase.
    • Genius bearing: Section 4(a)(11) bars the issuer from paying holders interest or yield solely for holding, using or keeping the coin; it takes effect with the Act (January 18, 2027 per the genius_act_milestones master). The OCC's proposed rule (not final) would presume the issuer is paying holders when it has an arrangement with an affiliate or related third party that in turn pays holders; whether that reaches Circle's revenue sharing with Coinbase has not been decided.
    Field guide cards
    Card: Name: Circle; Title: Circle starts from its regulated USDC coin and is expanding into payments, a blockchain and patents; Status: USDC live · move to a U.S. issuer not finished · IBM patents acquired; When: Liability → full stack; Cat: issuer-stack; State: public-filing; Careful: Buying IBM's blockchain patent portfolio did not give Circle COBOL, IBM mainframes or bank customer systems.; F: What it owns, USDC and EURC, CCTP (Cross-Chain Transfer Protocol), Gateway, CPN (Circle Payments Network), wallet tools, Arc development, USYC administration and a newly acquired IBM blockchain patent portfolio., The gates it sits on, Issuer liability to holders, moving coins between blockchains, institutional settlement and its own purpose-built blockchain, Where it can say no, Who can mint or redeem, supported blockchains, token addresses and compliance actions. Separate processors and blockchains keep their own approvals., The legal entities, Circle Internet Group, Inc. is the public parent. Circle Internet Financial, LLC is the current issuer outside the European Economic Area (EEA); Circle Internet Financial Europe SAS is the EEA issuer. The proposed Circle Internet Trust Company is the intended New York issuer. Circle National Trust has final approval from the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) as a separate custody and trust company that does not issue coins. Circle Technology Services runs CPN technology without becoming the principal party to transactions., Its money products, USDC and EURC are payment coins. USYC is a token for fund shares. The Arc network's asset and its infrastructure are separate things., How it makes money, Reserve income makes up most revenue. Circle reported Q1 2026 reserve income of $652.5 million versus $41.6 million of other revenue. It also reported $405.4 million of distribution and transaction costs, including large payments to distributors. Platform, network, fund and future Arc income are secondary., Its control levers, Issuer contracts, token-admin functions, Circle Mint onboarding, CCTP and Gateway support, CPN participation and the Arc roadmap., Who it depends on, Circle relies on the BlackRock-managed Circle Reserve Fund, BNY as custodian, partner banks, supported blockchains, distributors such as Coinbase and Binance, and payment processors., Its role in stablecoins and DeFi, USDC is the leading regulated base coin under exchanges, DeFi (decentralized finance) collateral, vaults and merchant payment services., Path to shops, Merchants can accept USDC directly through processors. USDC can also settle or pay out behind a familiar checkout without the shopper seeing it., Under GENIUS, Strong chance of approval under the GENIUS Act. But the exact move to a U.S. issuer and product-specific approval are separate legal steps., Latest, Circle National Trust received final OCC approval on 2026-07-10, but it is not the issuer. Circle acquired the IBM patent portfolio on 2026-07-27; we have not found a public list of the patents. The proposed New York issuer and the transfer of USDC contracts to it (novation) are not yet proven., Open questions, Which IBM patents cover bank integration, settlement among approved parties only, or enterprise controls? When will New York issuer terms line up with Arc and CPN production?, How it can break, A reserve-bank or custodian problem, an outage in issuer access, a token-admin error, a bridge or routing failure, or a mismatch when legal duties move to a new issuer. · Note: Stablecoin Field Guide company card for Circle (the USDC issuer group), kept whole. New facts here versus this row: the $405.4 million Q1 2026 distribution and transaction costs, the IBM blockchain patent portfolio (acquired 2026-07-27), 'Where it can say no' and 'How it can break'. The card's src entries (including one IBM explainer page) are the page's links and are not promoted to this row's sources.
    Sources: circle.com · Circle — USDC page ('$72.5B USDC in circulation as of 15 October 2026') (2026-10-15) · Circle — USDC Terms (last updated 2026-10-15) (2026-10-19) · Circle — Circle Granted Trust Charter by NYDFS (2026-07-31) · OCC — Circle national trust charter consummation record (2026-07-24) · Circle — final OCC approval for national trust bank (2026-07-10) · Circle Q1 2026 Form 10-Q (2026-03-31) · Circle Q2 2026 Form 10-Q — Coinbase distribution costs (2026-06-30) · Coinbase Help — USDC rewards overview (2026-10-19) · GENIUS Act, Public Law 119-27 (2025-07-18) · Circle IBM patents · Circle Arc · IBM COBOL

    USD₮ (USDT) · TetherLive

    Checked through: 2026-10-19 · Entity: USDT, Tether group · Also shown on: Freeze power, Where the interest goes, Issuer and reserves
    Peg
    USD
    Kind
    payment stablecoin candidate
    Issuer legal entity
    Tether International, S.A. de C.V.
    Parent company
    Tether group (Tether Operations S.A. de C.V. holds the brand and operating roles)
    Issuer jurisdiction
    El Salvador
    Genius route
    foreign issuer
    Genius route basis
    The issuer is foreign, so U.S. platforms can keep offering it after July 18, 2028 only if Treasury finds El Salvador's regime comparable, the issuer registers with the OCC, holds U.S. reserves for U.S. customers and can obey lawful orders; its reserve mix is a further hurdle.
    Charter or license
    Authorized by El Salvador's National Commission of Digital Assets (CNAD) as a stablecoin issuer and digital-asset service provider (library, July 2026). No U.S. registration or Treasury comparability finding was found.
    Reserves
    • Composition: Broader than the GENIUS list. Q2 2026 attestation (as of June 30, 2026): total assets $187.75 billion against $183.62 billion of liabilities for issued tokens, an excess of $4.11 billion; mostly U.S. government-backed instruments and short-term liquidity, plus more than 146 tons of gold and secured loans. Q1 2026 figures: about $141 billion of direct and indirect Treasury-bill exposure, about $20 billion of physical gold and about $7 billion of bitcoin.
    • Custodians: Several third-party banks and licensed financial institutions (not named publicly)
    • Latest attestation: By: BDO (quarterly attestation); separately, KPMG US audited the 2025 financial statements (opinion signed August 13, 2026); Date: 2026-06-30
    Redemption
    Only customers Tether approves after identity checks can redeem directly; most holders use exchanges, market makers and wallets.
    Market size
    • Usd: 183,622,105,630
    • As of: 2026-06-30
    • Basis: digital tokens issued by Tether International (all its fiat-backed tokens, not USD₮ alone), per BDO's assurance report
    Live since
    2014-10-06
    Status
    live
    Freeze control
    • Can freeze: Yes
    • Who: Tether (the issuer)
    • How: Tether can freeze and burn tokens on chains it supports and cooperates with law-enforcement seizures (library company card; Tether's own seizure announcements).
    Library records
    • genius_stablecoin_cards.json#usdt
    • company_control_cards.json#tether
    • genius_control_plane_cards.json#usdt-reserve,tether-novation
    Evidence through
    2026-10-19
    Verified on web
    Yes
    Web checked
    Q2 2026 attestation, KPMG audit, market size
    Open questions
    • The Q3 2026 attestation (as of Sep 30) was not yet located on 2026-10-19.
    • The Q2 page gives no dollar split for Treasuries, gold and bitcoin; only Q1 does.
    • The Q2 page cites about $184.6 billion of tokens issued while the balance sheet shows $183.62 billion of token liabilities; the gap is not explained.
    • Chain list not compiled from an official source.
    Yield
    • Holder paid interest: No
    • Where reserve income goes: To Tether. Tether reported about $1.5 billion of net operating profit for Q2 2026, driven by reserve and investment returns.
    • Genius bearing: The Section 4(a)(11) ban also binds foreign payment stablecoin issuers, so it would apply to Tether if USD₮ uses the foreign route.
    Sources: tether.io · Tether — BDO assurance report on Tether International's financial figures, June 30, 2026 (2026-06-30) · Tether — supports Royal Thai Police and U.S. Secret Service in seizing $12 million (2026-10-19) · Tether — Q1 2026 reserve report summary (2026-03-31) · Tether — KPMG audit of 2025 financial statements (2026-08-13) · Tether International — regulatory entity document (2026-07-28) · Tether — legal terms (2026-07-28) · GENIUS Act, Public Law 119-27 (2025-07-18)

    USA₮ (USAT) · USA₮Live

    Checked through: 2026-10-19 · Entity: USA₮, Anchorage Digital · Also shown on: Freeze power, Where the interest goes, Issuer and reserves
    Peg
    USD
    Kind
    payment stablecoin candidate
    Issuer legal entity
    Anchorage Digital Bank, N.A.
    Parent company
    Anchor Labs, Inc. (Anchorage Digital)
    Brand or distributor
    Tether Operations S.A. de C.V. licenses the USA₮ trademark; it does not issue the coin
    Issuer jurisdiction
    United States
    Genius route
    occ national trust
    Genius route basis
    The issuer is an uninsured national trust bank, which applies to the OCC as a federal qualified issuer; no GENIUS approval has been granted yet because the rules are not final.
    Charter or license
    Anchorage Digital Bank, N.A., a national trust bank chartered by the OCC in 2021 (the first digital-asset national trust charter); the OCC ended its 2021 operating agreement on February 9, 2026. The charter is not itself GENIUS approval.
    Reserves
    • Composition: Reserve assets are owned by the USA₮ Reserve Trust, a South Dakota express trust with Anchorage as trustee, kept separate from the bank.
    • Custodians: Anchorage Digital Bank, N.A. (trustee of the USA₮ Reserve Trust), Cantor Fitzgerald (designated reserve custodian and preferred primary dealer)
    • Latest attestation: By: A Big Four accounting firm (not named on Anchorage's page); Date: 2026-08-31
    Redemption
    Only eligible Anchorage bank clients can mint or redeem directly, 1:1 minus disclosed fees; other holders rely on markets and distributors.
    Live since
    2026-01-27
    Status
    live
    Freeze control
    • Can freeze: Yes
    • Who: Anchorage Digital Bank, N.A. (the issuer)
    • How: Under Anchorage's product terms the bank can refuse minting or redemption and freeze, block, seize or migrate tokens and restrict accounts (library company card).
    Library records
    • genius_stablecoin_cards.json#usat
    • genius_control_plane_cards.json#usat-reserve
    • company_control_cards.json#anchorage
    • charter.json 2025-12-01
    Evidence through
    2026-10-19
    Verified on web
    Yes
    Web checked
    latest attestation month
    Open questions
    • Current USA₮ supply: not stated on Anchorage's page; the figures sit in its PDF attestation reports.
    • Reserve income split between Anchorage and Tether.
    • Chains.
    Yield
    • Holder paid interest: No
    • Where reserve income goes: Not disclosed. Anchorage earns custody, issuance and reserve-management fees; how reserve income is split with Tether is not public.
    • Genius bearing: Section 4(a)(11) bars the issuer from paying holders interest or yield solely for holding, using or keeping the coin; it takes effect with the Act (January 18, 2027 per the genius_act_milestones master).
    Sources: anchorage.com · Anchorage — USA₮ policies and disclosures (2026-07-28) · Tether — USA₮ launch (2026-01-27) · OCC — termination of Anchorage's 2021 operating agreement (2026-02-09) · Anchorage — comment letter on OCC GENIUS proposal (describes issuance model) (2026-07-28) · GENIUS Act, Public Law 119-27 (2025-07-18)

    PYUSD · PayPal USDLive

    Checked through: 2026-10-19 · Entity: PYUSD, Paxos · Also shown on: Freeze power, Where the interest goes, Issuer and reserves
    Peg
    USD
    Kind
    payment stablecoin candidate
    Issuer legal entity
    Paxos Trust Company, N.A.
    Parent company
    Paxos group
    Brand or distributor
    PayPal; PayPal Digital, Inc. holds the customer crypto contract and pooled custody of customers' tokens but does not issue PYUSD
    Issuer jurisdiction
    United States
    Genius route
    occ national trust
    Genius route basis
    Paxos Trust Company is an uninsured national trust bank, which applies to the OCC as a federal qualified issuer; its existing charter is not a GENIUS approval.
    Charter or license
    Paxos Trust Company, N.A.: converted from a New York limited-purpose trust to a national trust bank, effective 5:00 p.m. December 12, 2025 (OCC); listed on the OCC's active trust-bank list April 30, 2026. Not FDIC-insured. A charter is not GENIUS approval.
    Reserves
    • Composition: U.S. dollar deposits (Paxos terms say FDIC-insured bank accounts), U.S. Treasury and other U.S. government-backed debt, and cash equivalents; monthly composition reports.
    • Custodians: Paxos Trust Company, N.A. (holds reserves; banks not named)
    • Latest attestation: By: KPMG LLP (monthly); Date: 2026-08
    Redemption
    Registered Paxos customers redeem 1:1 with no Paxos redemption fee (bank fees may apply; minimum may equal the bank's wire fee). PayPal and other platforms offer conversion for their own users.
    Market size
    • Usd: 2,886,948,845
    • As of: 2026-08-31
    • Basis: redeemable tokens outstanding, per KPMG's attestation (published by Paxos)
    Live since
    2023-08-07
    Status
    live
    Freeze control
    • Can freeze: Yes
    • Who: Paxos (the issuer)
    • How: Paxos can freeze tokens or their backing dollars, temporarily or permanently, with or without notice; can freeze and upgrade all tokens at once; and can seize tokens under a formal legal directive and destroy frozen or seized tokens. Grounds include regulator, court or law-enforcement directives, a partner's formal notice, or Paxos's sole discretion.
    Library records
    • genius_stablecoin_cards.json#pyusd
    • company_control_cards.json#paxos-paypal
    • genius_control_plane_cards.json#paypal-digital,paypal-distributor
    Evidence through
    2026-10-19
    Verified on web
    Yes
    Web checked
    terms (issuer, freeze, redemption), attestation firm, PayPal rewards, market size
    Open questions
    • Current PYUSD reward rate (shown only inside the PayPal app).
    • Reserve-income split between Paxos and PayPal.
    • Chain list not compiled from an official source.
    Yield
    • Holder paid interest: No
    • Where reserve income goes: To Paxos (Paxos charges a market-rate fee on reserves and shares earnings with distributors under contract; the split with PayPal is not public).
    • Rewards via third parties: PayPal runs PYUSD Rewards: opt-in, variable rate, accrued daily on the average PYUSD balance held at PayPal and paid monthly in PYUSD. Paxos terms also allow incentives to partners that promote or list its coins.
    • Genius bearing: Section 4(a)(11) bars the issuer from paying holders interest or yield solely for holding, using or keeping the coin; it takes effect with the Act (January 18, 2027 per the genius_act_milestones master). PYUSD is issued by Paxos on PayPal's behalf; the OCC's proposed rule (not final) would presume issuer-paid yield where the issuer has an arrangement with such a 'related third party' that pays holders, so balance-based PayPal rewards are squarely in question.
    Sources: paxos.com · Paxos — PYUSD attestation, August 31, 2026 (2026-08-31) · Paxos — stablecoin terms and conditions (last updated 2026-06-30) (2026-10-19) · PayPal — What are PYUSD Rewards? (2026-10-19) · PayPal — cryptocurrency terms (PayPal Digital role) (2026-07-28) · OCC news release — conditional approvals and Paxos conversion (NR 2025-125) (2025-12-12) · OCC — active national trust banks list (2026-04-30) · OCC proposed GENIUS rule (91 FR 10202), proposed 12 CFR 15.10(c)(4) (2026-03-02) · GENIUS Act, Public Law 119-27 (2025-07-18)

    USDG · Global DollarLive

    Checked through: 2026-10-19 · Entity: USDG, Paxos · Also shown on: Freeze power, Where the interest goes, Issuer and reserves
    Peg
    USD

    Parent company
    Paxos group

    foreign issuer
    Genius route basis
    Both issuers are foreign, so continued U.S. distribution after July 18, 2028 needs a Treasury comparability finding for the issuer's home regime, OCC registration, U.S.-held reserves and lawful-order capability.
    Charter or license
    Paxos Digital Singapore Pte. Ltd. holds a Monetary Authority of Singapore Major Payment Institution licence for digital payment token services; the EEA issuer is separately supervised (supervisor not named in the library).
    Reserves
    • Composition: Cash and cash equivalents including U.S. Treasury bills, in segregated accounts.
    • Custodians: Paxos issuing entities (segregated accounts; banks not named)
    • Latest attestation: By: KPMG (from February 2026, per library)
    Redemption
    Institutions create and redeem 1:1 directly with Paxos.

    • Basis: redeemable tokens outstanding, per KPMG Singapore's attestation (published by Paxos)

    2024-11
    Status
    live
    Freeze control
    • Can freeze: Yes
    • Who: Paxos (the issuer)
    • How: Paxos can freeze tokens or their backing dollars, temporarily or permanently, with or without notice; can freeze and upgrade all tokens at once; and can seize tokens under a formal legal directive and destroy frozen or seized tokens. Grounds include regulator, court or law-enforcement directives, a partner's formal notice, or Paxos's sole discretion.

    OUSD · Open USDLive

    Checked through: 2026-10-19 · Entity: OUSD, Bridge, Stripe · Also shown on: Freeze power, Where the interest goes, Issuer and reserves
    Peg
    USD
    Kind
    payment stablecoin candidate
    Issuer legal entity
    Bridge Building Inc.
    Parent company
    Stripe (acquired Bridge, closed February 4, 2025)
    Brand or distributor
    Open Standard (Open Standard LLC in the library) coordinates the product and partner economics; founding partners Coinbase, Mastercard, Shopify, Stripe and Visa; distribution through BVNK, Stripe, Visa, Coinbase, Privy and Mastercard routes
    Issuer jurisdiction
    United States
    Genius route
    unresolved
    Genius route basis
    Today the issuer is a nonbank Stripe company licensed as a money transmitter; it could seek the OCC federal-qualified route, a state route, or move issuance into the proposed Bridge National Trust Bank, which has only preliminary conditional approval.
    Charter or license
    Bridge Building Inc. operates under state money-transmission licensing (NMLS ID 2450917); its terms say the tokens are regulated as stored value. Bridge National Trust Bank received preliminary conditional OCC approval February 12, 2026 (Corporate Decision 1365); not opened, and it does not issue OUSD.
    Official ids
    Occ corporate decision: 1365
    Reserves
    • Composition: As of October 19, 2026: $99.5 million cash (12.1%) and $726.0 million in Treasuries, including government money-market funds holding Treasury-bill ladders under three months (87.9%); reserves $827.5 million against 825.5 million OUSD. Terms permit Treasury bills of three months or less, overnight Treasury reverse repo, government money-market funds, deposits and tokenized forms of these.
    • Custodians: BlackRock (named by Open Standard), Lead Bank (named by Open Standard), BNY (named in library launch records; not named on the current Open Standard post)
    • Latest attestation: By: A Big Four firm (not named); the dashboard lists an 'Aug 2026' report even though OUSD launched Sep 30 — see open questions; Date: 2026-08
    Redemption
    Holders who are eligible Bridge users redeem 1:1, processed within two business days of Bridge's compliance check, less applicable fees; partners advertise no-cost 1:1 mint and burn. Holders have no direct property interest in the reserves.
    Market size
    • Usd: 825,505,182
    • As of: 2026-10-19
    Live since
    2026-09-30
    Status
    live
    Chains
    Tempo, Solana, Base, Ethereum
    Freeze control
    • Can freeze: Yes
    • Who: Bridge Building Inc. (the issuer)
    • How: Bridge may blacklist addresses, restrict transfers and burn tokens if required by law or its internal compliance policies, with or without notice, and may suspend service on listed grounds (Bridge stablecoin terms, Sections 4 and 5.4). The fetched terms page covers 'Bridge Stablecoins' without naming OUSD; the library ties these terms to OUSD.

    Open questions
    • The dashboard lists an August 2026 attestation that predates OUSD's September 30 launch; is it a Bridge-wide report? Is there an OUSD-specific attestation?
    • Is BNY a reserve institution? Library launch records say yes; the current Open Standard post names only BlackRock, Lead Bank and 'other large financial institutions'.

    Yield
    • Holder paid interest: No
    • Where reserve income goes: To Bridge: its terms say Bridge keeps net reserve returns above the required reserve minimum.
    • Rewards via third parties: Open Standard says founders and participating partners can earn equity in proportion to the OUSD supply and activity they drive.
    • Genius bearing: Section 4(a)(11) bars the issuer from paying holders interest or yield solely for holding, using or keeping the coin; it takes effect with the Act (January 18, 2027 per the genius_act_milestones master). Partner equity tied to supply is an indirect arrangement of the kind the OCC's proposed affiliate/related-third-party presumption examines; not decided.

    Card: Name: SOFID; Title: SoFi's terms already allow it to move SOFID to a different issuer; Status: clause in current terms · not yet used; When: Operative contract; Cat: issuer-handoff; State: operative-contract; Careful: The clause shows SoFi is prepared; it does not prove the transfer has happened.; F: Who decides, SoFi Bank, and an approved successor or subsidiary, under the contract, Over what, SOFID issuer rights and duties, and holders' claims, When it switches on, SoFi names or assigns a new issuer in response to the new stablecoin law, The rule, SoFi's current terms allow an added or substitute issuer and the transfer of issuer obligations., What they can do, Move issuance, redemption and reserve duties into an approved subsidiary that is an insured bank., What changes, The app and business payment services can stay the same while the legal debtor changes., What you'd notice, Same ticker and app; a notice of the new issuer and revised terms., What a shop would notice, No required change to hardware or acceptance., The common mistake, A substitute issuer is announced, but holder liabilities are not actually transferred., Proof to look for, Approval as a permitted payment stablecoin issuer (PPSI), a signed assignment or novation, updated terms and reserve disclosures. · Note: Stablecoin Field Guide 'change of issuer' card for SOFID, kept whole.     • Where reserve income goes: To the reserve structure and Anchorage/Ethena under contract (split not public).
    • Rewards via third parties: Anchorage Digital Neo Ltd., a Cayman affiliate, pays rewards on the same USDtb under a separate contract while the coin stays transferable.
    • Genius bearing: Section 4(a)(11) bars the issuer from paying holders interest or yield solely for holding, using or keeping the coin; it takes effect with the Act (January 18, 2027 per the genius_act_milestones master). Rewards from an affiliate of the issuer are exactly what the OCC's proposed presumption targets (not final).
    Sources: anchorage.com · Anchorage — USDtb attestation report, August 31, 2026 (2026-08-31) · Anchorage — USDtb covered stablecoin terms (2026-07-28) · Anchorage — stablecoin rewards (2026-07-28) · OCC proposed GENIUS rule (91 FR 10202), proposed 12 CFR 15.10(c)(4) (2026-03-02) · GENIUS Act, Public Law 119-27 (2025-07-18)

    USDS · Sky DollarLive

    Checked through: 2026-07-28 · Entity: Sky Dollar · Also shown on: Not a payment stablecoin, Freeze power
    Peg
    USD
    Kind
    defi protocol dollar
    Brand or distributor
    Sky protocol (formerly Maker); Skybase International runs an interface and says it is not the issuer
    Genius route
    not a payment stablecoin
    Genius route basis
    No legal person says it owes holders cash on redemption; Sky calls USDS a 'Protocol Obligation', which leaves the issuer question open, and no regulator has ruled.
    Classification confidence
    likely outside no ruling
    Reserves
    Composition: Collateral in vaults: outside stablecoins (swappable through the peg stability module, e.g. into USDC), crypto, credit and real-world assets including Treasuries.
    Redemption
    Holders convert between DAI and USDS and swap into USDC through the peg stability module; no named issuer redeems for dollars.
    Market size
    Note: Removed 2026-10-19 under the primary-source rule (it came from DefiLlama). Every Sky-branded page says it is not the protocol's own documentation, and their figures disagree; no issuer-published figure.
    Status
    live
    Freeze control
    • Can freeze: unknown
    • Who: SKY token-holder governance
    • How: USDS contracts are upgradeable by governance vote; whether holder addresses can be frozen was not established in the library.
    Not payment stablecoin reason
    The Act's definition (Sec. 2(22)) needs an issuer legally obligated to redeem for a fixed amount of money; USDS has no named issuer that owes holders money; the peg stability module swap is a software conversion, not a legal promise to pay.
    Related tokens
    sUSDS (savings token)

    Yield
    • Holder paid interest: No
    • Where reserve income goes: Protocol income from lending and real-world assets funds savings rates, rewards, expenses and token buybacks, as governance decides.
    • Rewards via third parties: sUSDS is a separate savings token that earns Sky's savings rate; plain USDS earns nothing automatically.
    • Genius bearing: The Section 4(a)(11) ban binds permitted and foreign payment stablecoin issuers only; if this token is not a payment stablecoin, the ban does not reach it, though other laws (securities, commodities, sanctions) still apply.

    USDe is held near $1 by hedged crypto and derivatives, its redemption is synthetic and pro rata rather than a narrow reserve paying face value, and U.S. users cannot mint or redeem; no regulator has ruled.
    Classification confidence
    likely outside no ruling
    Reserves
    • Composition: Crypto and liquid-staking tokens (BTC, ETH, SOL), offset by short derivatives positions, plus stablecoins and stable reserve assets.

    Not payment stablecoin reason
    Backed by hedged crypto rather than the cash-and-Treasury reserves the Act lists, redeemable only by approved non-U.S. Mint Users, and designed as a synthetic dollar; Ethena's terms call it stored value, not a claim on Ethena.

    2 · The July card decks (archived pages; data still live)

    Issuer readiness: nine dollar stablecoins against the GENIUS Act

    · 9 rows

    The GENIUS Act does not make a blockchain or wallet legal. It makes a specific issuer, with specific reserves and terms, eligible to issue in the U.S. Later, it lets U.S. platforms keep distributing that issuer's coin. Existing payment companies and integrations can keep operating through the transition. But the token they carry must meet the issuer, reserve, redemption and lawful-order requirements.

    Read from: Research Packages/Fintech and Private Rails/Retail Payment Terminal Transition 2026-07-28/genius-stablecoin-issuer-readiness/genius_stablecoin_cards.json

    Circle plans to move USDC to a new New York issuer and its reserves to a new federal trust bank

    Card date: 2018-09-26 · Entity: USDC
    Route
    State-qualified issuer
    Evidence state
    Live, with independent attestations
    Symbol
    USDC
    Name
    USD Coin
    Date
    2018-09-26
    Status
    Live with current issuer · planned move to a New York issuer · federal trust bank for reserves
    Issuer
    Circle Internet Financial, LLC is still the issuer outside the European Economic Area (EEA). Circle told the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) it plans to move USDC issuance to a separate company. That company is Circle Internet Trust Company, LLC, a proposed New York limited-purpose trust. Circle Internet Financial Europe SAS remains the EEA issuer.
    Issuer obligation
    Eligible Circle Mint customers can create and redeem USDC 1:1. A holder gets a direct claim only by becoming an eligible Mint customer. Individuals generally get USDC through wallets, exchanges and neobanks.
    Reserve
    Highly liquid dollar reserves, held separately for holders: bank deposits, short-dated Treasuries and overnight Treasury repo. Most are held through the Circle Reserve Fund, managed by BlackRock.
    Reserve fit
    The asset types closely match the GENIUS Act's list. But no public determination exists that the reserves meet the rules for a permitted payment stablecoin issuer (PPSI). Circle's own disclosure is still the evidence.
    Custody
    BlackRock manages the Circle Reserve Fund, and BNY is the primary reserve custodian. Circle National Trust is designed to manage reserves, act as collateral trustee and hold digital assets in the future. It expressly does not issue USDC.
    Redemption
    Direct 1:1 redemption is for institutions, through Circle Mint. Everyday holders normally sell or redeem through an intermediary.
    Distribution
    Wallets, exchanges, banks, card networks and payment processors already distribute USDC widely. The companies that connect USDC to these systems are already established.
    Genius gate
    Circle's stated plan is a real change of issuer, to Circle Internet Trust Company, LLC. That company would then seek approval as a State-qualified PPSI under a New York regime certified under GENIUS. Using today's issuer, CIF LLC, as a federal nonbank issuer is only a fallback if the plan changes.
    Structural move
    Circle Internet Trust needs a charter and stablecoin approval from the New York State Department of Financial Services (NYDFS). It also needs the required GENIUS state certification and issuer approval. Circle must then sign an assignment and assumption or new terms. Token administration, reserve-control agreements and attestations must move to the new issuer.
    July 2028
    If the issuer becomes permitted, U.S. digital-asset service providers can keep offering the coin. Existing integrations do not by themselves meet the issuer requirement.
    Treasury channel
    USDC already places the money behind circulating coins in deposits, Treasury bills and Treasury repo. GENIUS would standardize this flow from private dollar tokens into government debt.
    Boundary
    Circle National Trust is not the planned issuer. Circle Internet Trust Company, LLC is a separate proposed New York company. We found no operative novation (legal transfer of the obligations) and no New York approval.
    Sources: Circle — USDC and redemption · Circle — reserve transparency · Circle — USDC terms · Circle — Mint and National Trust roles · OCC — Circle three-box business plan · NYDFS — Circle Internet Trust filing

    PayPal sells PYUSD, but Paxos is the company that owes holders the dollar

    Card date: 2023-08-07 · Entity: PYUSD
    Route
    Uninsured national bank
    Evidence state
    Live, with independent attestations
    Symbol
    PYUSD
    Name
    PayPal USD
    Date
    2023-08-07
    Status
    Live · monthly reserve reports and KPMG attestations
    Issuer
    Paxos Trust Company, N.A. issues and redeems PYUSD. PayPal Digital, Inc. holds the customer crypto contract and the wallet and pooled (omnibus) custody relationship. PayPal brands and distributes the coin.
    Issuer obligation
    Paxos manages issuance and redemption. Paxos states that PYUSD is fully backed and redeemable 1:1 for U.S. dollars.
    Reserve
    Paxos reports reserves made up of U.S. dollar deposits, U.S. Treasury instruments and cash equivalents. It publishes monthly composition reports and third-party attestations.
    Reserve fit
    The disclosed reserve types closely match the GENIUS list. Exact maturity, custody, diversification and operating requirements still await final rules and supervisory review.
    Custody
    Paxos is responsible for holding the PYUSD reserves. PayPal Digital holds customers' tokens as their agent in pooled (omnibus) accounts, directly or through a service provider. Holding customers' tokens is not holding the reserves.
    Redemption
    Paxos customers can convert PYUSD to dollars. PayPal and other platforms offer secondary access and conversion.
    Distribution
    PayPal, Venmo and public blockchains give PYUSD a very large wallet base. That does not move the issuer's obligation to PayPal.
    Genius gate
    Paxos Trust Company, N.A. is an uninsured national bank. It fits the application path for a Federal qualified issuer and must get approval from the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) to issue under GENIUS.
    Structural move
    Paxos must get approval as a permitted payment stablecoin issuer (PPSI). It must also bring its existing reserve, reporting, redemption, capital, risk and lawful-order programs in line with final rules.
    July 2028
    If Paxos becomes a permitted issuer, PYUSD keeps its U.S. wallet and exchange access. PayPal's distribution strength cannot replace Paxos's approval.
    Treasury channel
    PYUSD turns PayPal-scale wallet balances into demand for cash, Treasury instruments and Treasury-backed liquidity.
    Boundary
    Paxos's current authority is its national trust-bank charter. That is not a completed GENIUS approval as a PPSI.
    Sources: Paxos — PYUSD transparency · Paxos — current stablecoin terms · Paxos — PYUSD documentation · PayPal — customer, custody and Paxos roles

    Paxos both issues and brands USDPLive · monthly reserve reports

    Card date: 2018-09-10 · Entity: USDP
    Route
    Uninsured national bank
    Evidence state
    Live, with independent attestations
    Symbol
    USDP
    Name
    Pax Dollar
    Date
    2018-09-10
    Status
    Live · monthly reserve reports
    Issuer
    Current Paxos terms name Paxos Trust Company, N.A. as the issuer and manager of USDP.
    Issuer obligation
    Paxos states that USDP is fully backed, legally protected and redeemable 1:1.
    Reserve
    Paxos describes its U.S.-dollar stablecoin reserves as cash and cash-equivalent instruments. It publishes reserve reports and attestations.
    Reserve fit
    The asset mix broadly matches the GENIUS list. A formal GENIUS approval and a review against the rules are still needed.
    Custody
    Paxos says reserve and customer assets are kept separate and bankruptcy remote under its arrangements.
    Redemption
    Paxos customers can convert USDP to dollars. Other holders may need an eligible Paxos account or an intermediary.
    Distribution
    USDP circulates on Ethereum and Solana. Its branded-wallet presence is smaller than PYUSD's.
    Genius gate
    Same path as PYUSD: Paxos, an uninsured national bank, applies as a Federal qualified issuer.
    Structural move
    Paxos can seek one federal issuer operating model for several branded coins. But each coin's reserve, disclosure and redemption program still needs controlled reporting.
    July 2028
    Approval of the actual issuer is what protects exchange and wallet access. An old ticker is not exempt because of its age.
    Treasury channel
    USDP creates the same demand for cash-like reserve assets, without a separate consumer-wallet brand.
    Boundary
    Older Paxos documents name a New York LLC. This card uses the current June 2026 terms, which name Paxos Trust Company, N.A. Historical documents should not quietly replace the current issuer record.
    Sources: Paxos — current stablecoin terms · Paxos — regulation and transparency · Paxos — USDP support record

    Standard Custody, a New York trust company, issues RLUSD, not Ripple

    Card date: 2024-12-17 · Entity: RLUSD, Standard Custody & Trust Company
    Route
    State-qualified issuer
    Evidence state
    Live, with independent attestations
    Symbol
    RLUSD
    Name
    Ripple USD
    Date
    2024-12-17
    Status
    Live · issued by a New York-supervised trust company · monthly attestations
    Issuer
    Standard Custody & Trust Company, LLC issues RLUSD. The New York State Department of Financial Services (NYDFS) supervises it as a limited-purpose trust company.
    Issuer obligation
    The issuer states that RLUSD is backed by segregated reserves. Redemption follows set procedures with service-level controls.
    Reserve
    U.S. dollars and other cash equivalents, held in segregated accounts. Ripple publishes monthly independent attestations.
    Reserve fit
    The disclosed liquid reserves fit the law in concept. The exact mix of permitted assets and GENIUS certification of the New York regime still decide the question.
    Custody
    BNY is the primary reserve custodian and provides transaction banking. Ripple's network and enterprise software are separate from Standard Custody's duties as issuer.
    Redemption
    Institutions redeem through the issuer's distribution program. Terms for direct access by the public are less visible than the reserve attestations.
    Distribution
    Ripple provides enterprise distribution and ledger integrations. Standard Custody carries the duty to issue.
    Genius gate
    Likely path: become a State qualified payment stablecoin issuer under a certified New York regime while staying below the $10 billion federal-supervision threshold. Or move to a federal path.
    Structural move
    Keep Standard Custody as issuer, BNY as reserve custodian and Ripple as brand and distributor. Get state approval under a certified regime. Monitor the $10 billion consolidated-issuance threshold.
    July 2028
    U.S. intermediaries can keep distributing the coin only if the issuer is permitted by then.
    Treasury channel
    RLUSD's short-term liquid reserves direct institutional demand for the token toward cash and government liquidity instruments.
    Boundary
    Ripple's corporate licenses, ledgers and proposed federal entities do not replace the current issuer named in the reserve disclosures.
    Sources: Ripple — RLUSD transparency · Ripple — BNY reserve-custody role · U.S. Code — State-qualified issuer

    SoFi's bank issues SOFID itself, and the law requires a separate subsidiary

    Card date: 2025-12-18 · Entity: SoFiUSD
    Route
    Subsidiary of an insured bank
    Evidence state
    Live, company disclosures only
    Symbol
    SOFID
    Name
    SoFiUSD
    Date
    2025-12-18
    Status
    Live · issued directly by an insured national bank
    Issuer
    SoFi Bank, N.A. says it issues SOFID directly. The bank is an insured depository institution (IDI) regulated by the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC).
    Issuer obligation
    SoFi says SOFID is intended to be redeemable 1:1. It states plainly that SOFID is not a deposit, not FDIC-insured, not bank-guaranteed and may lose value.
    Reserve
    Mostly cash. Launch material says SOFID is fully reserved 1:1 to support immediate redemption. It cites cash held at the central bank as an advantage.
    Reserve fit
    Cash is the simplest permitted reserve type. The harder question is which legal entity issues the coin.
    Custody
    SoFi's bank systems control issuance, app distribution and reserve operations. The public product record does not identify a separate approved issuer subsidiary.
    Redemption
    SoFi provides 1:1 conversion for eligible users and partners.
    Distribution
    The SoFi app, enterprise services and potential white-label integrations with banks and fintechs.
    Genius gate
    Under GENIUS, the permitted bank issuer is an approved subsidiary of an insured depository institution, not the insured bank itself.
    Structural move
    SoFi would move or novate (legally transfer) issuance, redemption and the reserve obligation into an approved SoFi Bank subsidiary. SoFi's terms already let it name a substitute issuer and assign obligations in response to new stablecoin rules.
    July 2028
    The SoFi app can stay the wallet. But the token in it must be issued by the permitted entity.
    Treasury channel
    A mostly cash reserve relies on Federal Reserve and bank liquidity rather than Treasury-bill yield. The final reserve mix can change.
    Boundary
    This is an inference from the law's structure. It is not a finding that SoFi's current issuance is unlawful before GENIUS takes effect.
    Sources: SoFi — SOFID product disclosure · SoFi — launch and reserve description · SoFi — SOFID issuer-substitution terms · U.S. Code — permitted issuer definition · U.S. Code — IDI subsidiary applications

    Tether's U.S. coin is issued by Anchorage, with reserves held in a separate trust

    Card date: 2026-01-27 · Entity: USA₮
    Route
    Uninsured national bank
    Evidence state
    Live, with independent attestations
    Symbol
    USA₮
    Name
    USA₮ / USAT
    Date
    2026-01-27
    Status
    Live · monthly attestations by a Big Four accounting firm
    Issuer
    Anchorage Digital Bank, N.A. is the sole issuer and the only party that owes redemption. Tether Operations S.A. de C.V. licenses the USA₮ trademark but does not issue the coin.
    Issuer obligation
    Anchorage issues and redeems only for its bank clients, 1:1 minus disclosed fees.
    Reserve
    The USA₮ Reserve Trust, a South Dakota express trust with Anchorage as trustee, owns the reserve assets and keeps them separate. Anchorage publishes monthly attestations.
    Reserve fit
    The product is expressly designed for the GENIUS era. But final approval as a permitted payment stablecoin issuer (PPSI) and legal reserve compliance are still future steps.
    Custody
    Anchorage is trustee of the USA₮ Reserve Trust. Cantor Fitzgerald is the designated reserve custodian and preferred primary dealer. Tether supplies the trademark, not the obligation to holders.
    Redemption
    Only eligible Anchorage clients can redeem directly. Other users rely on markets and distributors.
    Distribution
    Built for U.S. institutional distribution, while Tether's global network stays centered on USD₮.
    Genius gate
    Anchorage Digital Bank is an uninsured national bank. It can seek approval as a Federal qualified payment stablecoin issuer.
    Structural move
    Anchorage must get the specific PPSI approval from the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC). It must also bring reporting, capital, risk, lawful-order and reserve operations in line with final rules.
    July 2028
    USA₮ is set up to keep U.S. intermediary access even if USD₮ uses the foreign-issuer path or fails it.
    Treasury channel
    A U.S. product with separate reserves lets Tether keep its global brand network. It also creates narrower domestic demand for U.S. government debt.
    Boundary
    The reserve trust holds the assets separately; it is not a second party that owes redemption. “Built for GENIUS” and a national bank charter do not prove that any GENIUS approval has happened yet.
    Sources: Anchorage — USA₮ policies · Anchorage — USA₮ reserve attestations · Tether — USA₮ launch

    USDG's reserves may meet U.S. rules, but its foreign issuer still needs U.S. recognition

    Card date: 2024-11-01 · Entity: USDG
    Route
    Foreign issuer
    Evidence state
    Live, with independent attestations
    Symbol
    USDG
    Name
    Global Dollar
    Date
    2024-11-01
    Status
    Live · issuer supervised by the Monetary Authority of Singapore (MAS) · monthly attestations
    Issuer
    Paxos Digital Singapore Pte. Ltd. and Paxos Issuance Europe Oy jointly manage and issue USDG, under separate foreign supervisors. The U.S. foreign-issuer review must identify which entity actually issued each token.
    Issuer obligation
    Paxos sells and redeems USDG 1:1. Reserves are held in segregated accounts.
    Reserve
    Cash and cash equivalents, including U.S. Treasury bills shown in reserve reports.
    Reserve fit
    The reserve assets appear to match closely. But GENIUS also tests the foreign regulatory regime, a U.S. liquidity reserve and registration, not only the portfolio.
    Custody
    Reserve assets sit in segregated accounts under the rules for the Singapore or European Economic Area (EEA) issuer. Global Dollar Network members distribute the coin but do not owe redemption.
    Redemption
    Paxos lets institutions create and redeem directly and convert to dollars.
    Distribution
    Global Dollar Network partners, wallets, exchanges and payment processors distribute the coin.
    Genius gate
    Foreign-issuer path, assessed one issuer at a time. Treasury must find the issuer's home regime comparable. That issuer must then register with the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC), hold enough U.S. reserves and comply with lawful orders.
    Structural move
    Choose and disclose the issuer that serves U.S. distribution. Win a comparability finding for its home regime and register that entity. Document its U.S. liquidity reserves and its ability to comply with lawful orders.
    July 2028
    Without the foreign exception, U.S. service providers must stop offering or selling USDG to U.S. persons on July 18, 2028.
    Treasury channel
    USDG already turns demand on foreign platforms into dollar cash and Treasury bills. GENIUS would add a liquidity reserve located in the U.S.
    Boundary
    Treasury has to formally find the home regime comparable. Supervision by MAS does not automatically count as U.S. recognition.
    Sources: Paxos — USDG documentation · Paxos — current stablecoin terms · U.S. Code — foreign exception

    Tether's main coin holds gold and bitcoin, which U.S. law does not allow as reserves

    Card date: 2014-10-06 · Entity: USDT, Bitcoin
    Route
    Foreign issuer
    Evidence state
    Live, with independent attestations
    Symbol
    USD₮
    Name
    Tether
    Date
    2014-10-06
    Status
    Live worldwide at large scale · quarterly BDO attestation
    Issuer
    Tether International, S.A. de C.V. issues USD₮ under authorization from El Salvador's National Commission of Digital Assets (CNAD). It is also a digital-asset service provider (DASP) and the party customers redeem with. Tether Operations S.A. de C.V. is a separate entity for the owner, brand and website roles.
    Issuer obligation
    Tether reports token-related liabilities and reserves that exceed them. Direct redemption runs through eligible Tether customer relationships.
    Reserve
    Mostly Treasuries, but broader than the GENIUS list. The Q1 2026 disclosure includes about $141 billion of direct and indirect Treasury-bill exposure, about $20 billion of physical gold and about $7 billion of Bitcoin, plus other assets.
    Reserve fit
    Gold and Bitcoin are not permitted reserve types under GENIUS. So the current portfolio cannot be assumed to meet the Act without restructuring, segregation or a different product.
    Custody
    The issuer's regulatory document says reserves sit with several third-party banks and licensed financial institutions, not one public custodian. It does not establish the U.S.-located liquidity reserve that the foreign exception requires.
    Redemption
    Only customers Tether approves can redeem directly. Most holders use exchanges, market makers and wallets.
    Distribution
    USD₮ has the world's largest stablecoin distribution network. That is its strength, and it is what the 2028 rule for U.S. intermediaries puts at risk.
    Genius gate
    Foreign-issuer path: a finding that the home regime is comparable, registration with the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC), liquidity reserves for U.S. customers and the ability to comply with lawful orders. The reserve mix is a further, substantive hurdle.
    Structural move
    Tether can qualify USD₮ through the foreign-issuer path with reserves that meet GENIUS. Or it can shift U.S. distribution to USA₮ while USD₮ stays global.
    July 2028
    U.S. wallets and exchanges cannot offer or sell the coin to U.S. persons after July 18, 2028 unless the foreign exception is met.
    Treasury channel
    USD₮ is already a major offshore source of demand for U.S. Treasuries. GENIUS makes continued U.S. distribution depend on placing the issuer and its reserves and terms under supervision that U.S. law recognizes.
    Boundary
    Reserves larger than liabilities is a different test. GENIUS asks whether every reserve asset is on its permitted list.
    Sources: Tether — Q1 2026 reserve report summary · Tether International — regulatory entity document · U.S. Code — distribution and treatment gates · U.S. Code — foreign exception

    Dollar tokens in DeFi: who owes the dollar

    · 17 rows

    A one-dollar target does not make every dollar token the same legal thing. Protocol-issued dollars, hedged (synthetic) dollars, payment coins, vault receipts and tokenized securities can sit in one wallet. Each can have a different issuer, collateral, controller and set of rules.

    Read from: Research Packages/Fintech and Private Rails/Retail Payment Terminal Transition 2026-07-28/defi-stablecoin-control-plane/defi_stablecoin_cards.json

    Whether a token is a payment stablecoin depends on how it is redeemed, not on its $1 price

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Type
    Legal classification question
    Evidence state
    Statute (enacted law)
    Token
    GENIUS §2(22)
    Status
    law enacted · how it applies to these tokens is not settled
    Dollar obligor
    A payment stablecoin must have an issuer obligated to convert, redeem or buy it back for a fixed amount of money.
    Stability engine
    The law separately requires the issuer to promise, or create a reasonable expectation of, a stable value.
    Control point
    Who exactly the legal issuer is, and what the holder receives on redemption
    Legal object
    A token qualifies only if it is built for payment or settlement, an issuer must redeem it, and redemption is for a fixed amount of money. Deposits and securities are excluded.
    Collateral
    What backs a token does not, by itself, decide whether it qualifies.
    Custody
    Redeeming for cash or a bank deposit differs from receiving USDC or one dollar's worth of crypto.
    Mint redeem
    A software swap, a peg stability module (PSM, a contract that swaps the token for another stablecoin) or a collateral redemption is not automatically a legal person's promise to pay money.
    Yield
    A permitted or recognized issuer may not pay yield just for holding, using or keeping the coin.
    Admin
    Issuer supervision, and the technical ability to obey lawful orders such as freezes, apply only once the product counts as a payment stablecoin.
    Genius read
    Each DeFi token must be tested against every part of the definition. Calling it a 'stablecoin' does not make it one.
    Distribution
    Falling outside the definition may avoid some GENIUS rules. It does not give the token the approval a permitted payment stablecoin issuer (PPSI) holds.
    Merchant path
    Payment processors will likely prefer the coin whose issuer and redemption rights are easiest to certify.
    Failure
    Treating a $1 price-feed (oracle) target or a USDC swap as dollar redemption under the law.
    Boundary
    Falling outside GENIUS does not mean unregulated. Securities, commodities, lending, sanctions and other laws still apply.
    Sources: Public Law 119-27

    Treasury's required study of these tokens has not been published

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Type
    Legal classification question
    Evidence state
    Legal status unresolved
    Token
    Treasury Section 14 study
    Status
    ten days past due · delivery not publicly confirmed
    Dollar obligor
    The study covers tokens that may have no ordinary payment-stablecoin issuer.
    Stability engine
    The law requires the study to cover reserves, algorithms, governance and decentralization.
    Control point
    Treasury writes it, after consulting the Fed, OCC, FDIC, SEC and CFTC
    Legal object
    A report to the Senate Banking and House Financial Services committees on stablecoins that are not payment stablecoins.
    Collateral
    The report must analyze what reserves hold and designs backed by the system's own tokens (endogenous designs).
    Custody
    Not specified. The product types and participants covered should show where institutional custody begins and ends.
    Mint redeem
    Expected to separate a software conversion from a legal right to redeem.
    Yield
    The study covers products whose economics do not fit payment coins, which pay no yield.
    Admin
    The law expressly requires the study to cover governance structure and decentralization.
    Genius read
    Through 2026-07-28, we found no public copy and no public confirmation that it was sent.
    Distribution
    How the study classifies these tokens can affect wallets, banks, processors and market utilities.
    Merchant path
    It determines whether protocol-issued dollars can gain institutional recognition or remain assets that must be converted before use.
    Failure
    Saying Treasury definitely failed, when it may have delivered the report privately to Congress.
    Boundary
    The law requires delivery to the committees, not public release.
    Sources: GENIUS Act §14

    The law bans interest on the coin itself, so interest moves to a separate productResult of how the law is built

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Type
    Legal classification question
    Evidence state
    Statute (enacted law)
    Token
    Base coin → yield claim
    Status
    result of how the law is built
    Dollar obligor
    The base payment coin's issuer still owes holders redemption at face value (par).
    Stability engine
    The base coin's reserve stays separate from any lending, fund, hedging or rewards income.
    Control point
    Whoever issues the separate token or account that holds the coin, or a non-issuer that pays rewards
    Legal object
    An asset split swaps the coin for a new vault, security or fund claim. An actor split keeps the same coin but changes who pays.
    Collateral
    The underlying payment coin may be lent out, locked in a vault or swapped for an investment.
    Custody
    The user may hand control of the base coin to a smart contract or platform.
    Mint redeem
    Withdrawal rights run against the new receipt or account, not automatically against the base coin's issuer.
    Yield
    The return comes from borrower interest, Treasury income, hedging, staking or incentives, not the issuer's reserve.
    Admin
    Protocol votes, vault managers and reward-program terms now decide how the product works.
    Genius read
    The separate token and the rewards program each need their own legal classification. Neither gets automatic protection.
    Distribution
    Institutional wallets can offer a no-yield coin and a separate earning product side by side.
    Merchant path
    The earning position is usually converted back to a permitted base coin before payment.
    Failure
    Calling sUSDe, USYC or an Aave receipt 'yield-bearing USDC.'
    Boundary
    Even if the money feels the same, a new legal or smart-contract claim has been issued.
    Sources: 12 U.S.C. §5903 · SEC crypto-asset release

    Sky (formerly Maker) runs DAI and USDS without a company that owes holders the dollarLive · unclear who the issuer is

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28 · Entity: Dai, Sky Dollar
    Type
    Dollar created by DeFi software
    Evidence state
    Live product documents
    Token
    DAI / USDS / sUSDS
    Status
    live · unclear who the issuer is
    Dollar obligor
    None of the Sky legal entities we reviewed says it owes holders cash on redemption. Whether a DAO (a group of token holders who vote) counts as a legal person is unresolved.
    Stability engine
    Collateral in vaults, swaps through the peg stability module (PSM), interest rates, liquidations and holder votes.
    Control point
    SKY token holders vote, and the USDS contracts can be upgraded
    Legal object
    Sky describes USDS as issued by the protocol and as a redeemable 'Protocol Obligation.' sUSDS is a separate savings share.
    Collateral
    Outside stablecoins, crypto, credit, and real-world assets including Treasuries.
    Custody
    Depends on the collateral and on how each Sky Agent is set up.
    Mint redeem
    Holders can convert DAI to USDS and back, and swap into USDC through the PSM. No named issuer redeems for dollars or deposits.
    Yield
    Plain USDS earns nothing automatically. sUSDS and the DAI Savings Rate (DSR) are separate savings positions.
    Admin
    Holder votes change collateral, rates and the PSM, and put approved changes into effect.
    Genius read
    Likely not a payment stablecoin. Sky's own claim wording leaves open who the issuer is.
    Distribution
    Widely used in DeFi and on exchanges. Companies that run the apps can add their own restrictions.
    Merchant path
    Merchants can already choose DAI payouts. A processor's support does not prove permitted payment stablecoin issuer (PPSI) status.
    Failure
    Treating a protocol claim or a swap into USDC as a legal person's promise to pay dollars.
    Boundary
    Sky's documents are authoritative on how the system works. They are not a U.S. regulator's classification.
    Sources: Sky — USDS · Sky — token routes · Sky — terms

    Aave's GHO is created by parties approved by a token-holder vote; no company owes holders the dollarLive · argued to fall outside the law

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28 · Entity: GHO
    Type
    Dollar created by DeFi software
    Evidence state
    Live product documents
    Token
    GHO / sGHO
    Status
    live · argued to fall outside the law
    Dollar obligor
    No named legal person owes holders cash or deposit redemption.
    Stability engine
    Collateral on Aave, minting limits for each Facilitator (a party approved to create GHO), the GHO Stability Module (GSM), interest rates and arbitrage.
    Control point
    Aave DAO (Aave token holders who vote), Facilitators, and GHO Stewards acting under delegated power
    Legal object
    A dollar-like token created by the protocol. sGHO is a separate savings receipt.
    Collateral

    Distribution

    Merchant path

    Failure

    Curve's crvUSD has no company issuer, but the contract that creates it is known

    Liquity's code cannot be changed, which also means it cannot be brought under the lawLive · structural changes require a new system

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28 · Entity: BOLD, LUSD
    Type
    Dollar created by DeFi software
    Evidence state
    Live product documents
    Token
    BOLD / LUSD
    Status
    live · structural changes require a new system
    Dollar obligor
    No named legal issuer, and no one owes holders cash or deposit redemption.
    Stability engine
    Loans backed by more collateral than they borrow, redemptions for crypto, the Stability Pool and liquidations.
    Control point
    Core contract rules that cannot be changed, plus actions by users and keepers
    Legal object
    Debt tokens created by the protocol. Stability Pool positions are separate claims with their own risk.
    Collateral

    Strong case that it falls outside the payment-stablecoin definition. It cannot meet permitted-issuer controls without a new system.

    Hedged (synthetic) dollar
    Evidence state
    Issuer's binding terms
    Token
    USDe / sUSDe
    Status
    live · no U.S. users may create or redeem

    Ethena BVI Limited, under current non-EEA terms. Only approved Mint Users have direct rights against it.

    Crypto and liquid-staking tokens (LSTs), offset by derivatives positions, plus funding and basis income and stable reserve assets.

    A stable price does not turn derivatives collateral into a reserve GENIUS allows.
    Sources: USDe terms · Ethena architecture · Ethena custody

    Evidence state
    Issuer's binding terms
    Token
    USDtb
    Status
    live payment coin · rewards under a separate contract
    Dollar obligor
    Anchorage Digital Bank, N.A. is the only issuer and owes redemption to eligible clients.
    Stability engine
    A South Dakota reserve trust, mostly BUIDL plus cash in the period reviewed.
    Control point
    Anchorage Bank controls the coin; Anchorage Digital Neo Ltd. controls the rewards
    Legal object
    Holders keep the same USDtb. No separate token or new receipt is issued for rewards.

    The trust and the reserve counterparties it discloses.

    Failure

    Agora calls its payments rewards for activity, but calculates them on daily balancesLive offshore · U.S. trust proposed

    Type
    Moving toward regulation
    Evidence state
    Issuer's binding terms
    Token
    AUSD
    Status
    live offshore · U.S. trust proposed
    Dollar obligor
    Agora Bermuda Limited, acting for the AUSD Segregated Account.
    Stability engine
    Separately held cash, Treasuries, reverse repurchase agreements, regulated stablecoins and tokenized fixed income.
    Control point
    Agora as issuer, plus its reserve partners and contract administrators
    Legal object
    Holders receive periodic loyalty payments on the same AUSD token.
    Collateral
    The terms allow more kinds of assets than the short GENIUS reserve list.
    Custody
    Agora's product materials name roles for State Street and VanEck.
    Mint redeem
    Verified business users only; U.S. users are barred. The terms allow delayed or in-kind redemption.

    The terms describe payments as rewards for activities and contributions, but calculate them from daily AUSD balances.
    Admin
    Agora can freeze, pause, upgrade, seize and destroy tokens, and control what happens on chain changes or forks.

    A candidate foreign payment-stablecoin issuer. Big questions remain about yield on the same token and whether its reserves qualify.
    Distribution
    Used in DeFi and in white-label partner coins. Partners' obligations stay separate.
    Merchant path
    Would need a recognized foreign issuer or a future U.S. issuer, plus processor support.

    M^0 names every company that controls M except one that owes all holders the dollarLive · legal terms differ by product

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Type
    Dollar created by DeFi software
    Evidence state
    Live product documents
    Token
    M
    Status
    live · legal terms differ by product
    Dollar obligor
    No single public legal person owes every ordinary M holder redemption for a fixed amount of money.
    Stability engine
    Approved Minters (parties allowed to create M), collateral in special purpose vehicles (SPVs), Validators and an interest-rate model.
    Control point
    Lists approved by holder vote, Validators and the MinterGateway contract
    Legal object
    One M contract supports both fixed balances and balances that grow automatically (rebasing) to pay earnings.
    Collateral
    Eligible collateral differs by product and Minter, held through separate SPV and custody arrangements.
    Custody
    Depends on the Minter. Disclosed examples include European institutional structures.
    Mint redeem
    MinterGateway is the only contract that can create or destroy M. Whether ordinary holders can redeem for cash is unresolved.
    Yield
    Approved addresses turn on earning (startEarning) on the same M token.
    Admin
    Holder votes approve or remove Minters and Earners. Validators can freeze Minters and cancel token creation.
    Genius read
    Someone controls M but no one owes holders the dollar, and the token itself pays earnings. Both make it a poor payment-stablecoin fit.
    Distribution
    Builders can wrap M in their own tokens or brand it, through many different legal issuers.
    Merchant path
    A licensed, product-specific token holding M can reach merchants. Generic M's status is not enough.
    Failure
    Assuming one shared protocol design means one issuer for every coin built on M^0.
    Boundary
    The technical power to create tokens and the legal duty to redeem them are separate.
    Sources: M^0 architecture · M token · M^0 roles

    USYC / YLDS

    Stability engine
    Returns from a fund portfolio or issuer debt, not a reserve paying face value.
    Control point
    The fund and token administrator, or the registered debt issuer and its transfer limits
    Legal object
    A new security, received in exchange for or instead of a payment coin.

    Fireblocks lets institutions lend stablecoins through Aave and Morpho to earn interestLive · company product announcement

    Type
    Wallet or processor that delivers tokens
    Evidence state
    Live product documents
    Token
    Stablecoin → Aave / Morpho
    Status
    live · company product announcement
    Dollar obligor
    The stablecoin's issuer still owes holders. Fireblocks does not take on that duty.
    Stability engine
    The issuer's reserve, plus separate lending and vault returns from the protocols.
    Control point
    Fireblocks' product and policy settings, and the protocol contracts it selects
    Legal object
    Institutions use their accounts to enter a new DeFi lending or vault position.
    Collateral
    The protocols' collateral and borrowers' positions.
    Custody
    Fireblocks wallet and custody settings, plus public contracts.
    Mint redeem
    The issuer still redeems the base coin. Withdrawing from the protocol is a separate step.
    Yield
    Returns come from Aave or Morpho borrowers or vaults, behind the Fireblocks app.
    Admin
    Institution permissions, transaction policies, protocol votes and emergency controls.

    Shows how a payment coin that pays no yield can feed a separate yield product.
    Distribution
    Institutional clients get DeFi exposure through wallet controls they already use.
    Merchant path

    Shift4 is the merchant's processor and payout service, not DAI's issuer.

    Legal object

    Collateral

    Some dollar tokens stay on the blockchain after the company behind them shut downShut down

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28 · Entity: USDM, USDL
    Type
    Legal classification question
    Evidence state
    Issuer's binding terms
    Token
    USDM / USDL
    Status
    shut down
    Dollar obligor
    Mountain and Paxos International ended their duties to create, reward and redeem under their wind-downs.
    Stability engine
    Leftover market trading or a move to another token replaced ongoing issuer support.
    Control point
    The issuer's wind-down terms, the migration process and remaining trading pools
    Legal object
    The token still exists technically, but its commercial and legal promise ended or moved.
    Collateral
