    Trading pools can continue after official support ends.
    Merchant path
    Processors should drop an unsupported token, even if its contract still allows transfers.
    Failure
    Claiming GENIUS caused either shutdown without direct evidence.
    Boundary
    A market price near one dollar does not bring back the issuer's duty to redeem.
    Sources: Mountain USDM wind-down · Paxos USDL wind-down

    What crypto companies control

    · 22 rows

    The stablecoin competition is not one coin against another. Companies are racing to control the steps around the coin: legal liability to holders, reserves, ledgers, keys, risk, conversion to regular money and reaching customers.

    Read from: Research Packages/Fintech and Private Rails/Retail Payment Terminal Transition 2026-07-28/defi-crypto-company-control-map/company_control_cards.json

    Circle starts from its regulated USDC coin and is expanding into payments, a blockchain and patents

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28 · Entity: Circle
    Type
    Issuer groups
    Evidence state
    Public filing
    Name
    Circle
    Status
    USDC live · move to a U.S. issuer not finished · IBM patents acquired
    Owns
    USDC and EURC, CCTP (Cross-Chain Transfer Protocol), Gateway, CPN (Circle Payments Network), wallet tools, Arc development, USYC administration and a newly acquired IBM blockchain patent portfolio.
    Gate
    Issuer liability to holders, moving coins between blockchains, institutional settlement and its own purpose-built blockchain
    Stop
    Who can mint or redeem, supported blockchains, token addresses and compliance actions. Separate processors and blockchains keep their own approvals.
    Legal entities
    Circle Internet Group, Inc. is the public parent. Circle Internet Financial, LLC is the current issuer outside the European Economic Area (EEA); Circle Internet Financial Europe SAS is the EEA issuer. The proposed Circle Internet Trust Company is the intended New York issuer. Circle National Trust has final approval from the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) as a separate custody and trust company that does not issue coins. Circle Technology Services runs CPN technology without becoming the principal party to transactions.
    Money objects
    USDC and EURC are payment coins. USYC is a token for fund shares. The Arc network's asset and its infrastructure are separate things.
    Economic engine
    Reserve income makes up most revenue. Circle reported Q1 2026 reserve income of $652.5 million versus $41.6 million of other revenue. It also reported $405.4 million of distribution and transaction costs, including large payments to distributors. Platform, network, fund and future Arc income are secondary.
    Control surface
    Issuer contracts, token-admin functions, Circle Mint onboarding, CCTP and Gateway support, CPN participation and the Arc roadmap.
    Dependencies
    Circle relies on the BlackRock-managed Circle Reserve Fund, BNY as custodian, partner banks, supported blockchains, distributors such as Coinbase and Binance, and payment processors.
    Stablecoin defi role
    USDC is the leading regulated base coin under exchanges, DeFi (decentralized finance) collateral, vaults and merchant payment services.
    Merchant path
    Merchants can accept USDC directly through processors. USDC can also settle or pay out behind a familiar checkout without the shopper seeing it.
    Genius read
    Strong chance of approval under the GENIUS Act. But the exact move to a U.S. issuer and product-specific approval are separate legal steps.
    Live clock
    Circle National Trust received final OCC approval on 2026-07-10, but it is not the issuer. Circle acquired the IBM patent portfolio on 2026-07-27; we have not found a public list of the patents. The proposed New York issuer and the transfer of USDC contracts to it (novation) are not yet proven.
    Open questions
    Which IBM patents cover bank integration, settlement among approved parties only, or enterprise controls? When will New York issuer terms line up with Arc and CPN production?
    Failure
    A reserve-bank or custodian problem, an outage in issuer access, a token-admin error, a bridge or routing failure, or a mismatch when legal duties move to a new issuer.
    Boundary
    Buying IBM's blockchain patent portfolio did not give Circle COBOL, IBM mainframes or bank customer systems.
    Sources: Circle USDC · Circle Q1 2026 filing · Circle IBM patents · Circle Arc · IBM COBOL

    Coinbase earns from distributing USDC and runs Base, but Circle issues the coin

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28 · Entity: Coinbase
    Type
    Customer distributors
    Evidence state
    Company disclosure
    Name
    Coinbase
    Status
    payments, custody and Base live · figures are self-reported
    Owns
    Exchange and custody platforms, Coinbase Developer Platform, payment APIs, wallets, x402 and its relationship with the Base network.
    Gate
    Reaching customers, custody, conversion to regular money, merchant APIs and Base as the preferred place to run transactions
    Stop
    Listings, customer access, custody support, payment methods, API access, and access to Base's sequencer (which orders transactions) and interface. Circle keeps USDC issuer powers.
    Legal entities
    Coinbase Global is the parent of regulated exchange, brokerage and custody subsidiaries. Base and each product role are separate; do not treat them as one legal person.
    Money objects
    Circle issues USDC. Coinbase distributes it and earns money under a commercial agreement. Other supported and custom stablecoins keep their own issuers.
    Economic engine
    Trading and custody fees, subscriptions and services, USDC-related income, payment infrastructure and activity tied to Base.
    Control surface
    Customer identity checks (KYC), supported assets, custody keys, merchant APIs and Base transaction ordering. Anyone can submit Base fault proofs, but upgrades need 9 of 12 Security Council members.
    Dependencies
    Coinbase relies on Circle to owe USDC holders, banks and card networks to move regular money in and out, Ethereum to settle Base, and outside processors and merchants.
    Stablecoin defi role
    A major holder and router of USDC. Links stablecoins to trading, DeFi (decentralized finance) on Base, institutional custody and payments by AI agents.
    Merchant path
    Coinbase Payments handles acceptance. Processors such as Checkout.com or PPRO keep the merchant relationship and the contract to settle in regular money.
    Genius read
    Likely a critical digital-asset service provider and distributor, even when it is not the issuer.
    Live clock
    Coinbase announced merchant acceptance and enterprise payouts as live for eligible users. Headline volume and Base figures are company-reported.
    Open questions
    In each processor integration, who exactly is responsible for compliance, conversion, custody and refunds? What is the status of the proposed trust company?
    Failure
    One platform outage can hit transfers, card transactions and onchain services at the same time. A failure in Base's sequencer or in custody would hit many services at once.
    Boundary
    Stablecoin volume on Base is not Coinbase revenue, USDC in circulation or merchant-payment volume. Open fault proofs do not remove Coinbase's control of the sequencer and interface.
    Sources: Coinbase Payments · Checkout.com integration · MassPay integration · Base Security Council

    Ripple owns the widest range of institutional services around its RLUSD stablecoinRLUSD live · set of acquired businesses growing

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Type
    Issuer groups
    Evidence state
    Current legal terms
    Name
    Ripple family
    Status
    RLUSD live · set of acquired businesses growing
    Owns
    Payments software, a central place in the XRP Ledger (XRPL) ecosystem, Standard Custody, Ripple Custody, Ripple Prime, Ripple Treasury and acquired wallet and payment systems.
    Gate
    Issuing a stablecoin, custody, prime brokerage, connecting corporate treasuries and routing institutional payments
    Stop
    Standard Custody controls RLUSD issuance and redemption. It can freeze, blacklist, burn or replace tokens and suspend redemptions. Product subsidiaries control access to custody, prime and treasury services. XRPL validators keep their network roles.
    Legal entities
    Ripple Labs Inc. is separate from Standard Custody & Trust Company, which issues RLUSD today. Ripple National Trust has only preliminary, conditional approval. Hidden Road Partners CIV US LLC—not Ripple Labs—holds the relevant memberships in FICC (Fixed Income Clearing Corporation) and DTC (Depository Trust Company). The Metaco/Palisade, Rail and GTreasury entities keep the specific roles they had when acquired.
    Money objects
    RLUSD (owed by its issuer), XRP (the ledger's native asset), third-party assets issued on XRPL, and customer securities and collateral are legally separate.
    Economic engine
    Payments, custody and software fees; prime brokerage and financing; treasury software; XRP holdings and markets; and RLUSD-related business.
    Control surface
    RLUSD token administration, customer onboarding, custody policies, prime-brokerage collateral, software integration, and market memberships held by specific acquired subsidiaries.
    Dependencies
    BNY for reserve custody and banking, other banks and exchanges, the specific subsidiaries that are DTC and FICC members, XRPL validators and outside blockchains such as Ethereum.
    Stablecoin defi role
    RLUSD is a regulated base coin for tokenized funds, DeFi (decentralized finance) and Ripple Payments. USDC also exists natively on XRPL.
    Merchant path
    Ripple Payments and the acquired Rail systems can convert between stablecoins and regular money. Evidence of direct use at consumer checkout terminals is more limited.
    Genius read
    RLUSD likely seeks approval as a state-qualified issuer through Standard Custody. Ripple National Trust is on a separate timeline.
    Live clock
    Standard Custody issuance, Ripple Prime, Ripple Treasury and Ripple Custody are live. Ripple National Trust has only preliminary, conditional approval. The deciding proof is repeated transactions that use several acquired businesses together.
    Open questions
    Which acquired connections become Ripple's own production systems? Does post-trade activity actually move to XRPL?
    Failure
    Too many services concentrated in one corporate group, a custodian or issuer failure, losses at the prime broker, a token-admin action, or a ledger or bridge incident.
    Boundary
    A subsidiary's connection to DTC, FICC, Swift, Canton or a bank does not prove that institutions have adopted XRP, XRPL or RippleNet.
    Sources: RLUSD terms · Ripple infrastructure · Ripple custody · USDC on XRPL

    Tether keeps global trading in USD₮ while Anchorage issues its separate U.S. coin, USA₮

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28 · Entity: USDT, USA₮
    Type
    Issuer groups
    Evidence state
    Current legal terms
    Name
    Tether
    Status
    USD₮ global · USA₮ a separate coin issued by Anchorage
    Owns
    The Tether brand, offshore token relationships and a broad investment and technology group. It licenses a separate U.S. product that Anchorage issues.
    Gate
    Liquidity on global exchanges, reserve management and token administration
    Stop
    Tether controls direct customer issuance and redemption, supported networks and token freezes. Exchanges and Anchorage separately control access to their products.
    Legal entities
    Tether International, S.A. de C.V. is the legal entity behind USD₮ after a documented 2025 assignment (legal transfer). Tether Operations owns and licenses the brand and operating intellectual property but does not issue USA₮. Anchorage Digital Bank issues USA₮ under a separate reserve trust.
    Money objects
    Offshore USD₮ and U.S. USA₮ share a brand but not an issuer, reserve or legal promise.
    Economic engine
    Reserve income, returns on its investment portfolio, issuance and redemption relationships, and group investments.

    Token freeze and burn, primary minting and redemption only after know-your-customer (KYC) checks, choice of blockchains, self-custody wallet distribution and reserve allocation.

    USD₮ is a leading base coin for trading and DeFi (decentralized finance). What its reserves hold differs materially from the assets the GENIUS Act permits.
    Merchant path
    It usually reaches merchants through conversion at an exchange, wallet or processor, not through direct retail redemption.
    Genius read
    Offshore USD₮ needs a foreign-issuer approval path or limits on its distribution. USA₮ is a separate regulated U.S. option.
    Live clock
    Watch whether U.S. access moves to USA₮ while global trading stays in USD₮.
    Open questions
    For each product: its exact status under GENIUS, its U.S. distribution policy and its reserve and custody details.
    Failure
    Losses at reserves or custodians, exchange delisting, a redemption backlog, a mismatch between legal entities, or token controls on a specific blockchain.
    Boundary
    USA₮'s Anchorage and Cantor structure does not apply to USD₮.
    Sources: Tether legal · Tether information document · Anchorage GENIUS letter

    Issuer groups
    Evidence state
    Current legal terms
    Name
    Paxos / PayPal
    Status
    PYUSD, USDP and USDG live, issued by different Paxos entities
    Owns
    Paxos runs regulated systems for issuing coins and tokenizing assets. PayPal controls the consumer wallet and the merchant channels that distribute the coin.
    Gate
    Issuing coins under other brands (white-label), running reserves and redemptions, and branded consumer access
    Stop
    Paxos controls minting, redemption and token administration. PayPal Digital controls customer support, the custody product customers see, and whether the product is available.
    Legal entities
    Paxos Trust Company, N.A. issues PYUSD and USDP under current terms. Paxos Digital Singapore and Paxos Issuance Europe manage and issue USDG. PayPal Digital holds the customer crypto contract and the rewards relationship but does not issue PYUSD. Paxos Securities Settlement Company is a separate clearing entity.

    Dependencies
    Banks and custodians holding reserves, PayPal and other distributors, supported blockchains and payment networks.
    Stablecoin defi role
    Paxos coins can move into wallets, exchanges and DeFi (decentralized finance), while Paxos remains the place to redeem.
    Merchant path
    Shoppers can pay with PYUSD at PayPal checkout. Other crypto may be converted to regular money at checkout, so the merchant is settled as usual.
    Genius read
    Paxos is well prepared to be an issuer. Product-specific approvals and the treatment of distributor payments remain open.
    Live clock
    Current terms dated 2026-06-30 name the issuing entities. Moving PayPal customer contracts did not change the issuer.
    Open questions
    How the limits on issuers paying yield treat Paxos's shared earnings and PayPal Digital's balance-based rewards. Which Paxos entity serves each jurisdiction.
    Failure
    Losses at the issuer or custodian, a distributor dropping the product, a blockchain admin action, or confusing the brand with the company that owes holders.
    Boundary
    PayPal does not owe PYUSD redemption just because the token carries its brand.
    Sources: Paxos terms · Paxos issuance · PYUSD docs

    Links balances to branded coins, in-app wallets and Morpho yield picked by curators.
    Merchant path
    A stablecoin balance or DeFi-funded wallet can pay through a regular card or Stripe checkout, while the merchant receives ordinary settlement.
    Genius read
    The issuer depends on the product. Stripe or Bridge may be distributor, payment router, issuer or program manager in different flows.
    Live clock
    Bridge, Privy and some stablecoin products are live. Tempo had a public test network. Stripe's Sessions 2026 announcements list generally available (GA), preview and future-quarter items separately.
    Open questions
    Who exactly issues and holds custody for each custom coin? How do liquidation and credit work when someone spends from a DeFi position?
    Failure
    One outage hitting payments, wallets and routing at once, a sponsor-bank failure, a smart-contract loss, or unclear treatment of who owns customer property.
    Boundary
    A roadmap item is not a live product. Bridge routing payments does not automatically mean Bridge issued the coin.
    Sources: Stripe Sessions 2026 · Stripe Treasury stablecoins · Ramp and Stripe

    Ethereum hosts stablecoins and other financial tokens but is not their issuer

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Type
    Blockchain networks
    Evidence state
    Protocol documentation
    Name
    Ethereum ecosystem
    Status
    live · several independent software clients · community-governed
    Owns
    No single body owns Ethereum. The Ethereum Foundation, software client teams, validators, node operators, standards authors, infrastructure firms and layer-2 (L2) network operators have different roles.
    Gate
    Running and settling transactions, smart-contract records and apps that plug into each other
    Stop
    Validators can censor transactions or leave them out. Client teams and the community can choose software and recovery plans. Application admins keep their own keys.
    Legal entities
    The Ethereum Foundation is a Swiss Stiftung (foundation) and an influential funder and organizer. It is not the protocol, the validators or an issuer. Consensys Software Inc. runs products such as MetaMask and Infura and contributes client software, but it does not own Ethereum.
    Money objects
    ETH pays transaction fees (gas) and secures consensus. USDC, PYUSD, USDe, GHO and tokenized securities are liabilities or claims of other systems.
    Economic engine
    ETH fees and burn, validator rewards and MEV (profit from ordering transactions), L2 income, RPC and wallet services, and application fees.
    Control surface
    EIP (Ethereum Improvement Proposal) debate, AllCoreDevs coordination, independent client builds, node and validator adoption, rare community-led recovery, and app and L2 upgrade keys.
    Dependencies
    Client diversity, validator concentration, RPC access, bridges and L2s, oracles (outside data feeds), app admins and stablecoin issuers.
    Stablecoin defi role
    The main network where issuer coins, lending, decentralized exchanges (DEXs), synthetic dollars, vaults and tokenized assets work together.
    Merchant path
    Usually through L2s, wallets, processors or crypto-linked cards, not direct retail use of the base layer-1 (L1) chain.
    Genius read
    The network does not decide whether a coin is a payment stablecoin. The exact issuer and its redemption promise do.
    Live clock
    Protocol upgrades need client teams to build them and validators and nodes to adopt them. No single company controls every layer.
    Open questions
    In practice, how concentrated are clients, staking providers, RPC providers, L2 sequencers and stablecoin admins?
    Failure
    Client bug, validator concentration, finality failure, community split, L2 or bridge failure, or a compromised app admin.
    Boundary
    The Ethereum Foundation (EF) does not own Ethereum, and Consensys does not control it. Validators do not formally vote on every EIP. Stablecoin value does not become EF revenue or liability.
    Sources: Nodes and clients · Validator FAQ · Ethereum security report · Ethereum governance

    Stellar treats reaching users, not the token, as the main constraint

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28 · Entity: Stellar
    Type
    Blockchain networks
    Evidence state
    Company disclosure
    Name
    Stellar / SDF
    Status
    cash conversion and stablecoin payments live · DTC connection not yet live
    Owns
    The Stellar Development Foundation (SDF) supports protocol development and ecosystem programs. Third-party issuers, anchors (cash-conversion companies), validators, wallets and MoneyGram keep separate roles.
    Gate
    Moving assets cheaply, standards for anchors, converting cash, and distributing regulated assets
    Stop
    Each issuer can set authorization, freeze or clawback-style controls on its asset. Anchors can refuse conversion. Validators choose which validators they trust (quorum sets) and vote on protocol versions and settings.
    Legal entities
    The Stellar Development Foundation is a nonprofit support body. It is not the issuer of USDC, PYUSD, MGUSD or tokenized funds.
    Money objects
    XLM pays fees and minimum balances (reserves). Third-party stablecoins and securities keep their own legal issuers.
    Economic engine
    Users need XLM for fees, rent and minimum balances. But fees go into a fee pool no one can access, and validators receive no protocol rewards. Anchors, wallets and applications earn separately.
    Control surface
    Protocol releases, validator quorum choices, SDF ecosystem funding, issuer settings on each asset, anchors and wallet integrations.
    Dependencies
    Issuers, the MoneyGram/Crossmint cash network, anchors, validators, wallets, card networks and a future DTC (Depository Trust Company) connection.
    Stablecoin defi role
    USDC, PYUSD and locally distributed stablecoins can connect cash, remittances, aid, wallets and tokenized assets.
    Merchant path
    MoneyGram and wallet apps convert cash to and from stablecoins. Visa/Wirex settlement and spending through apps offer other paths.
    Genius read
    SDF is normally infrastructure, not a payment-stablecoin issuer. Each asset on Stellar needs its own legal test.
    Live clock
    Cash conversion and payments are live. The DTC connection targets H1 2027 (first half). The Protocol 26/CAP-77 emergency ledger-key quarantine must be confirmed on mainnet before we call it live.
    Open questions
    How wider distribution turns into lasting income for the network and token, and how issuer controls work with Soroban smart contracts.
    Failure
    Anchor insolvency, issuer freeze, validator quorum fault, wallet failure or trading split across too many places.
    Boundary
    SDF is not a bank or stablecoin issuer. XLM is not reserve backing. A DTC connection does not replace DTC's own books. Validators are not paid like miners or stakers.
    Sources: Distribution thesis · MoneyGram impact · Q1 2026 report

    Hedera offers businesses built-in tools that let issuers control their own tokens

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28 · Entity: Hedera
    Type
    Blockchain networks
    Evidence state
    Protocol documentation
    Name
    Hedera
    Status
    network and tools live · range of real production use still uneven
    Owns
    A network whose rules and ownership the Hedera Council governs, plus ecosystem tools. Stablecoin Studio is an open-source kit that third-party issuers use to issue coins.
    Gate
    Council-run consensus, built-in token tools, predictable fees and business workflows mixing public and private systems
    Stop
    The Council governs network software, treasury and pricing. Token issuers set their own keys for identity checks (KYC), freezing, pausing, wiping balances and supply.
    Legal entities
    Hedera Hashgraph, LLC dba (doing business as) Hedera Council is the Delaware entity that governs the network. Hedera Foundation and Hashgraph are independent ecosystem and software organizations. Hiero is the open-source project. All are separate from token issuers.
    Money objects
    HBAR pays public-network fees and secures consensus. Stablecoins, tokenized collateral and state-issued tokens are claims on third parties.
    Economic engine
    Network fees paid in HBAR and ecosystem activity. Issuers and applications earn separately.
    Control surface
    Council decisions govern treasury, pricing, membership, releases and mainnet-node policy. Only Council members currently run mainnet consensus nodes. Each token is controlled by keys its issuer sets in the Hedera Token Service (HTS): admin, supply, KYC, freeze, wipe, fee and pause.
    Dependencies
    Council continuity, nodes, outside issuers, custodians, reserve-data providers and oracles (outside data feeds) such as Chainlink.
    Stablecoin defi role
    Stablecoin Studio provides connection points for compliance and proof of reserves. Hedera Token Service provides built-in controls over a token's whole life.
    Merchant path
    Issuer and payment-provider apps can use Hedera. Merchant acceptance still runs through wallets, processors and acquirers (merchants' payment banks).
    Genius read
    Stablecoin Studio may help an issuer meet control rules. It cannot supply the legal issuer, rights to reserves or approval.
    Live clock
    Wyoming FRNT and commercial projects show real deployment. Institutional pilots still need confirmation that they are in production.
    Open questions
    Which uses repeat at scale, and how public Hedera connects with private HashSphere networks.
    Failure
    Council or node concentration, stolen keys, an issuer-admin action, an oracle or custody fault, or private and public networks falling out of sync.
    Boundary
    Open-source Hiero code does not let anyone join mainnet without approval. Council governance does not make Hedera the issuer of every token made with its tools.
    Sources: Stablecoin Studio · Studio launch · HBAR economics

    Chainlink's data and cross-chain messages can affect settlement, but it owns no asset

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Type
    Service providers / custody
    Evidence state
    Protocol documentation
    Name
    Chainlink
    Status
    data-feed (oracle) and CCIP services live · rules for institutional use incomplete
    Owns
    Software, standards and business relationships for data feeds, proof of reserve, CCIP (Cross-Chain Interoperability Protocol) and automated workflows.
    Gate
    Verified data, messages between blockchains and transactions that run only when conditions are met
    Stop
    Each service's proxy owners and admins, DON (decentralized oracle network) settings, CCIP rate limits and emergency controls can halt or change a service. Chainlink does not usually control the reserve or the official record of the asset.
    Legal entities
    Chainlink Labs and the ecosystem and foundation bodies are separate from independent node operators and customer protocols.
    Money objects
    LINK pays for and backs parts of the service economics. Assets arriving on another chain remain issuer or protocol claims.
    Economic engine
    Service fees, the option to pay in other tokens that convert to LINK, and the Chainlink Reserve, plus limited staking and security mechanisms. Official v0.2 staking has a 45 million LINK cap and launched around the Ethereum ETH/USD feed—not every Chainlink service.
    Control surface
    Service settings, which nodes and operators serve, data sources, CCIP routes between chains, emergency procedures and customer integration contracts.
    Dependencies
    Data providers, node operators, sending and receiving blockchains, protocol admins and institutional counterparties.
    Stablecoin defi role
    Prices collateral, supports proof-of-reserve designs and moves instructions and tokens between blockchains, including GHO routes and RLUSD data.
    Merchant path
    Indirect. It supports reliable conversion, risk checks and cross-chain settlement that wallets and processors use.
    Genius read
    Likely an intermediary or service provider, not an issuer. Legal duties depend on the exact service and its control over transfers.
    Live clock
    It is used in production. But public terms on liability, corrections and recovery for institutional workflows remain limited.
    Open questions
    Which services staking secures, who bears losses from bad data, and how corrections for institutions are decided.
    Failure
    Wrong or outdated data, nodes colluding, a cross-chain hack, a misconfigured route or unclear liability.
    Boundary
    An oracle integration is not issuance or custody. LINK does not back stablecoins moved between chains. Staking does not currently secure every service.
    Sources: Chainlink economics · RLUSD price feeds · Aave RemoteGSM

    Failure

    Boundary

    Protocols run by governance votes
    Evidence state
    Protocol documentation
    Name
    Aave
    Status
    protocol and GHO live
    Owns
    No ordinary parent owns the protocol. Aave DAO (decentralized autonomous organization, run by token votes) governs deployments and GHO; Aave Labs builds software and interfaces.
    Gate
    Collateral-backed loans, liquidity and how much GHO each approved minter (facilitator) may create
    Stop
    Governance can add or remove facilitators and change caps. Stewards can adjust rates, exposures and freezes within limits governance gave them.
    Legal entities
    Aave DAO and community, Aave Labs (with its interface and trademark roles), independent risk and technical service providers, stewards and GHO facilitators are different actors. Aave Labs' terms deny that it controls the protocol.

    GHO is a dollar the DAO controls. Aave also provides the lending and yield underneath regulated coins.
    Merchant path
    Most likely a wallet or processor converts GHO, or a card service lets someone spend against an Aave or Morpho position.
    Genius read
    Strong argument that GHO is not a payment stablecoin, since no named company owes fiat redemption. Approval for institutional use remains uncertain.
    Live clock
    RemoteGSM (remote GHO Stability Module) and a modular design spread GHO across more chains; minting still comes only from governance-approved sources.
    Open questions
    Whether the DAO is treated as a person or issuer, facilitators' legal liability, and processor acceptance.
    Failure
    An oracle or collateral shock, a takeover of governance, a facilitator fault, failed liquidations or a smart-contract exploit.
    Boundary
    Aave Labs is not automatically the issuer or operator of every DAO deployment.
    Sources: GHO facilitators · Aave community and stewards · GHO introduction

    Name
    Sky / Maker lineage
    Status

    Gate
    Which collateral is accepted, how many dollars exist, the savings rate and the move to the new protocol
    Stop
    Governance can change settings and contract code. Interfaces can restrict users. Collateral custodians keep their own controls over each asset.

    USDC and other collateral issuers, oracles, custodians, governance voting, market liquidity and front ends.

    DAI and USDS can be distributed or converted. But a processor supporting them does not create a company that owes holders regular money.
    Genius read
    Likely not a payment stablecoin. Ambiguity comes from Sky's official 'protocol obligation' language and the law's broad definition of a person.
    Live clock
    The move from DAI to USDS changes the economics, who controls it and its legal identity.
    Open questions
    Whether the law treats the DAO or governance as an issuer or person, and which entity owns specific RWA claims.
    Failure
    Collateral losing its peg, governance takeover, an oracle or PSM failure, a custodian or SPV problem, or a contract migration error.
    Boundary
    Swapping for USDC is not necessarily redemption for monetary value as the law defines it.

    Normally converted into payment money first. Direct payment use is limited by securities eligibility rules and transfer controls.
    Genius read
    The GENIUS Act excludes securities, so USDY and OUSG fall outside the payment-stablecoin definition.
    Live clock
    Some product and blockchain integrations are live. Where descriptions of the issuer conflict, the current legal terms control.
    Open questions
    How far tokenized securities can go into checkout, collateral and card products while keeping securities controls.
    Failure
    An issuer, agent or custodian problem, market loss, eligibility failure, bridge or control bug, or redemption delay.
    Boundary
    A dollar-like net asset value (NAV) or instant conversion does not make the security a payment stablecoin.
    Sources: USDY · USDY legal notes · OUSG

    Morpho's vault curators decide where lenders' money goes, doing the intermediary's job

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Type
    Investment / yield
    Evidence state
    Protocol documentation
    Name
    Morpho
    Status
    open lending markets anyone can create and curator-managed vaults live
    Owns
    Morpho Association runs interfaces and ecosystem services. Morpho Labs is a separate development company. Control is split among governance and product-specific actors, across lending markets that cannot be changed and separately managed vaults.
    Gate
    Lending markets anyone can create, how vault money is allocated, and which yields are chosen
    Stop
    Governance cannot pause or rewrite existing Morpho markets. Vault owners, curators, allocators and sentinels can choose markets, allocate funds and use limited product powers.
    Legal entities
    Morpho Association is a French nonprofit association. Morpho Labs is a separate development company. Token governance, market creators and independent vault curators, allocators and sentinels are separate actors.
    Money objects
    The underlying stablecoin is still owed by its issuer. The lender's or vault's receipt becomes a separate credit claim.
    Economic engine
    Borrowing interest, curator and vault fees, protocol rewards, and income from interfaces and integrations.
    Control surface
    Market settings, choice of price oracle, vault curation and allocation, caps, guardians and distribution through front ends.
    Dependencies
    Stablecoin issuers, borrowers, collateral, price oracles, liquidators, curators and wallet and card distributors.
    Stablecoin defi role
    A source of yield increasingly built into institutional wallets and Stripe/Privy-style products.
    Merchant path
    A card or wallet sells, borrows against or moves a Morpho position into spendable money before the merchant is paid.
    Genius read
    A Morpho receipt or vault claim needs its own analysis. The base payment coin's issuer status does not protect the yield product.
    Live clock
    Watch curator concentration and whether card and wallet integrations reach production.
    Open questions
    Which legal entity owes users in curated products, how bad debt is shared out and who controls emergency actions.
    Failure
    Bad debt, oracle failure, curator or allocation error, lack of liquidity, a flaw in a market that cannot be changed, a smart-contract exploit, or a front-end or custody failure.
    Boundary
    Anyone can create a market, but that does not mean every vault is unmanaged or equally safe.
    Sources: Morpho docs · Stripe Sessions 2026

    M^0 shows clearly who controls minting, but not who owes holders

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Type
    Service providers / custody
    Evidence state
    Legal category unresolved
    Name
    M^0
    Status
    M protocol live · unclear who owes ordinary holders
    Owns
    Protocol governance assigns minter, validator and earner roles. Each product's minters hold collateral in SPVs (special purpose vehicles).
    Gate
    Minting only by approved minters, confirmation of collateral, and programmable sharing of earnings
    Stop
    Governance can approve or remove roles. Validators can freeze minters and cancel mint proposals. MinterGateway controls minting and burning on the blockchain.
    Legal entities
    M^0 Foundation is a Swiss foundation that supports governance and ecosystem infrastructure. It is not automatically the issuer of every coin. Independent minters, SPVs, validators, earners, Stablecoin Core issuers and Extension administrators have separate responsibilities.
    Money objects
    The M base token can have fixed or rebasing (self-adjusting) balances. Branded extensions can add another liability and distribution layer.
    Economic engine
    Interest that minters owe, payouts to earners, protocol and governance income, and branded-issuance services.
    Control surface
    Approved lists for each role, validator attestations and freezes, MinterGateway contracts, each product's Stablecoin Core, Extension admins, bridges, the rate model and distribution vaults.
    Dependencies
    Minters staying solvent, SPV collateral, validators, custodians, governance and distributors.
    Stablecoin defi role
    Back-end money system for branded coins and balances that earn in the same token.
    Merchant path
    Usually through a branded distributor or processor, not with M as a visible retail asset.
    Genius read
    A clear control system does not by itself name one legal entity that owes redemption. Yield paid in the same token complicates payment classification.
    Live clock
    Product-specific legal documents are the deciding piece still missing.
    Open questions
    Who owes ordinary M holders regular money, who owns the collateral, and how failures are resolved across minters.
    Failure
    A minter or SPV default, a wrong attestation, too few validators in control, a gateway bug or a governance failure.
    Boundary
    Being able to switch issuers technically does not move the legal duty to redeem, the reserve property or the user contract. One token contract does not create one issuer for everyone.
    Sources: M^0 architecture · M token · M^0 roles

    Evidence state
    Current legal terms

    AUSD live outside the U.S. · national trust bank proposed

    Minting and redemption for corporate users, a segregated reserve, token administration and distribution through partners

    Under its terms, Agora can freeze addresses, pause or upgrade, seize or destroy tokens, and choose how to treat chains and forks.

    Liquity's core contracts have no administrator, so they cannot be paused or upgraded

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Type
    Protocols run by governance votes
    Evidence state
    Protocol documentation
    Name
    Liquity
    Status
    BOLD and LUSD live · core contracts cannot be changed
    Owns
    Liquity AG develops and supports the software. Core contracts that cannot be changed run borrowing, redemptions and liquidations, with no administrator who can upgrade them.
    Gate
    Borrowing against more collateral than the loan, and redemption for crypto collateral
    Stop
    The core design has no way to pause, upgrade, blacklist or move by governance vote. Front ends and third parties can still restrict access.
    Legal entities
    Liquity AG develops and supports software but does not run one official front end. Independent interfaces, the unchangeable contracts, and users and liquidators are separate. No named issuer that owes regular money is documented.
    Money objects
    BOLD and LUSD are protocol debt assets. Stability Pool positions are separate.
    Economic engine
    Borrower interest, gains from liquidations, Stability Pool returns and activity on third-party interfaces.
    Control surface
    Core contract rules, collateral types fixed at launch, user positions, liquidators and independent interfaces.
    Dependencies
    Ethereum, outside collateral and liquid staking token (LST) issuers, oracles and market liquidity, liquidators and front ends.
    Stablecoin defi role
    A protocol dollar strongly built to resist censorship. It fits poorly with institutions' expectation that coins follow lawful orders.
    Merchant path
    Must be swapped on the market for a coin a processor supports.
    Genius read
    Strong case that it falls outside the payment-stablecoin definition. Permitted-issuer compliance would need a successor system.
    Live clock
    Any structural compliance change must come in new contracts or products, not a retrofit of the existing ones.
    Open questions
    How the law treats unchangeable protocols, and practical distribution when intermediaries keep approved lists.
    Failure
    Collateral, oracle or liquidity failure, chain disruption or interfaces disappearing. No administrator can step in to rescue it.
    Boundary
    Unchangeable contracts do not remove sanctions, interface, collateral-issuer or market risk.
    Sources: BOLD · Redemptions · Risk disclosure

    GENIUS control plane: twenty legal events that can change a coin

    · 20 rows

    A stablecoin can keep the same ticker, wallet and payment terminal while its issuer, its reserve holder or its permission to be sold in the U.S. changes. Each card names who holds the legal power, what that party can change and what event lets it act.

    Read from: Research Packages/Fintech and Private Rails/Retail Payment Terminal Transition 2026-07-28/genius-control-plane/genius_control_plane_cards.json

    Each company that issues a coin must be approved; the token itself is notEnacted · effective date not yet fixed

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Track
    Dates set by the statute
    Evidence state
    Statute (enacted law)
    Entity
    U.S. payment-stablecoin issuer
    Status
    enacted · effective date not yet fixed
    Controller
    Federal or qualifying State regulator
    Controlled object
    The legal entity that issues the dollar claim in the United States
    Trigger
    18 Jan 2027 or 120 days after final implementing rules, whichever comes first
    Legal role
    After the general effective date, only a permitted payment stablecoin issuer (PPSI) may issue in the United States.
    Action
    The regulator approves, denies or sets conditions on the exact legal entity that issues the coin.
    Downstream effect
    Minting must happen in an approved entity, even if the brand, blockchain, wallet and processor stay the same.
    Customer view
    Possibly no visible change beyond revised terms.
    Merchant view
    Merchants can keep the same payment method if the coin behind it stays eligible.
    Failure mode
    Mistaking a licensed affiliate, custodian or parent company for the issuer.
    Proof needed
    A regulator approval naming the exact issuer, plus token terms in force naming that same entity.
    Boundary
    A charter alone is not PPSI approval under GENIUS.
    Sources: 12 U.S.C. §5902

    Once the law takes effect, an unapproved coin loses its cash-like treatment at banks and brokers

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Track
    Dates set by the statute
    Evidence state
    Statute (enacted law)
    Entity
    Banks, brokers, clearing firms and payment systems
    Status
    enacted · applies before the 2028 platform deadline
    Controller
    The statute, plus each regulated institution
    Controlled object
    Treatment as a cash equivalent, collateral, margin or wholesale settlement asset
    Trigger
    General effective date
    Legal role
    Section 5902(g) removes specified institutional-money treatment from a coin that is not permitted.
    Action
    Institutions reclassify or reject the asset in their accounting, collateral and settlement policies.
    Downstream effect
    Banks and payment networks may shorten their approved-coin lists well before 18 Jul 2028.
    Customer view
    Retail trading may continue while banks and other institutions stop handling the coin.
    Merchant view
    A processor can drop a settlement option without changing the terminal hardware.
    Failure mode
    Treating 2028 as the first deadline with real economic effect.
    Proof needed
    The final effective date, plus revised policies from banks, networks, custodians and clearing firms.
    Boundary
    The statute does not automatically erase a token from a public blockchain.
    Sources: 12 U.S.C. §5902(g)

    A foreign issuer must be able to freeze or burn coins on a U.S. orderEnacted · earlier condition for foreign issuers

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Track
    Government and court orders
    Evidence state
    Statute (enacted law)
    Entity
    Foreign payment-stablecoin issuer
    Status
    enacted · earlier condition for foreign issuers
    Controller
    Treasury and the foreign issuer
    Controlled object
    Whether the issuer is able to comply with lawful orders
    Trigger
    General effective date, plus a path to offer or trade the coin in the U.S.
    Legal role
    A foreign coin requires the technical ability to comply with lawful orders, and actual compliance.
    Action
    The issuer shows it can freeze, seize, burn or block transfers of coins when ordered.
    Downstream effect
    A foreign coin that its issuer cannot technically control can lose access to U.S. intermediaries.
    Customer view
    Transfers may be blocked, or a custodial platform may drop the asset.
    Merchant view
    Processors can turn off the asset and keep other payment types.
    Failure mode
    Assuming strong reserves alone satisfy the foreign-issuer path.
    Proof needed
    Issuer policy, administrator controls, a tested procedure and regulator acceptance.
    Boundary
    Having this ability does not mean blanket surveillance or automatic seizure.
    Sources: 12 U.S.C. §5901 — lawful order · 12 U.S.C. §5907

    If a foreign issuer ignores a U.S. order, Treasury can stop U.S. trading of its coinEnacted · enforcement in set steps

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Track
    Government and court orders
    Evidence state
    Statute (enacted law)
    Entity
    Treasury → covered U.S. providers
    Status
    enacted · enforcement in set steps
    Controller
    Secretary of the Treasury
    Controlled object
    U.S. secondary trading of the named foreign stablecoin
    Trigger
    Foreign issuer fails to comply with a lawful order
    Legal role
    Treasury may find noncompliance, allow time to fix it, publish its decision and bar U.S. providers from facilitating the coin.
    Action
    Written designation → 30 days to cure → Federal Register notice → 30-day delay → ban on providers.
    Downstream effect
    Exchanges, custodial wallets and other covered providers stop supporting trading.
    Customer view
    Trading, deposits or transfers can be restricted in stages.
    Merchant view
    Processors turn off payment acceptance or settlement for the coin in their settings.
    Failure mode
    Describing the process as an instant, blockchain-wide ban.
    Proof needed
    Treasury designation, cure record, Federal Register determination and provider notices.
    Boundary
    The ban reaches covered intermediaries; it does not stop the underlying protocol.
    Sources: 12 U.S.C. §5907

    From July 18, 2028, U.S. platforms may offer only approved coinsDate fixed by statute

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Track
    Who may sell or offer the coin
    Evidence state
    Statute (enacted law)
    Entity
    U.S. digital-asset service provider
    Status
    date fixed by statute
    Controller
    Each covered U.S. digital-asset service provider, as the statute requires
    Controlled object
    Which payment stablecoins it may offer or sell to U.S. persons
    Trigger
    18 Jul 2028
    Legal role
    A digital-asset service provider (DASP) may offer or sell only a permitted coin, or one that meets the foreign exception.
    Action
    List, keep, restrict, wind down or remove each asset.
    Downstream effect
    Wallets, exchanges and issuance-service firms set coin eligibility in their product settings.
    Customer view
    The coin may vanish from buy and sell menus, while self-custodied balances remain.
    Merchant view
    The payment method can disappear from a processor dashboard without replacing the terminal.
    Failure mode
    Calling the rule a protocol ban, or assuming it covers every piece of software.
    Proof needed
    Provider listing policy, supported-asset records, customer notices and regulator guidance.
    Boundary
    The statute's definitions expressly exclude protocols, self-custody software, validators and liquidity-pool participation.
    Sources: 12 U.S.C. §5901 — DASP definition · 12 U.S.C. §5902(b)(1)

    SoFi's terms already allow it to move SOFID to a different issuerClause in current terms · not yet used

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28 · Entity: SoFiUSD
    Track
    Change of issuer
    Evidence state
    Contract in force
    Entity
    SOFID
    Status
    clause in current terms · not yet used
    Controller
    SoFi Bank, and an approved successor or subsidiary, under the contract
    Controlled object
    SOFID issuer rights and duties, and holders' claims
    Trigger
    SoFi names or assigns a new issuer in response to the new stablecoin law
    Legal role
    SoFi's current terms allow an added or substitute issuer and the transfer of issuer obligations.
    Action
    Move issuance, redemption and reserve duties into an approved subsidiary that is an insured bank.
    Downstream effect
    The app and business payment services can stay the same while the legal debtor changes.
    Customer view
    Same ticker and app; a notice of the new issuer and revised terms.
    Merchant view
    No required change to hardware or acceptance.
    Failure mode
    A substitute issuer is announced, but holder liabilities are not actually transferred.
    Proof needed
    Approval as a permitted payment stablecoin issuer (PPSI), a signed assignment or novation, updated terms and reserve disclosures.
    Boundary
    The clause shows SoFi is prepared; it does not prove the transfer has happened.
    Sources: SoFi — SOFID terms · 12 U.S.C. §5904

    Change of issuer

    USD₮
    Status
    completed issuer transfer (past event)

    Tether Limited and Tether International, S.A. de C.V.
    Controlled object
    All rights, duties and liabilities under the token contract, and user claims
    Trigger
    Token terms effective 27 Jan 2025, and users' continued use

    The old issuer assigned the token relationship to the new issuer, and users released the old entity.
    Action
    Transfer the legal obligation without requiring retail users to use a new app or wallet.
    Downstream effect
    The ticker and blockchain can stay the same while the company that owes redemption changes.

    Who issues USDC, and who manages and holds its reserves

    A New York limited-purpose trust is set up and approved, and issuance actually moves to it
    Legal role

    USD₮'s reserves include assets the U.S. law does not allow

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Track
    Who holds the reserves
    Evidence state
    Current company disclosure
    Entity
    USD₮ reserve
    Status
    broad reserve in place · foreign-issuer approval pending
    Controller
    Tether International, with multiple banks and licensed institutions
    Controlled object
    A broad reserve portfolio backing USD₮
    Trigger
    Issuance and redemption, and any move to use the GENIUS foreign exception
    Legal role
    Tether International is the issuer and owes the reserves. Disclosed counterparties hold or bank the reserve assets.
    Action
    Keep liquidity now. Possibly narrow, separate and place enough reserves in the U.S. for the foreign-issuer path.
    Downstream effect
    Large Treasury holdings can coexist with asset types that GENIUS would require Tether to restructure.
    Customer view
    The world's most liquid stablecoin may face a different U.S. path from USA₮.
    Merchant view
    A processor can support USA₮ domestically while restricting USD₮.
    Failure mode
    Treating U.S. Treasury holdings as proof that the full reserve meets GENIUS.
    Proof needed
    Issuer-specific reserve report, a comparability decision, Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) registration and evidence of U.S. reserves.
    Boundary
    USA₮ is a separate product with a separate obligor. It does not automatically continue every USD₮ claim in the U.S.
    Sources: Tether — relevant information document · Tether — Q1 2026 reserve summary

    The power to freeze coins may sit with a token administrator, not the reserve bankRequirement for how controls are designed

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Track
    Government and court orders
    Evidence state
    Statute (enacted law)
    Entity
    Issuer + token administrator
    Status
    requirement for how controls are designed
    Controller
    The issuer's compliance team and an authorized token administrator
    Controlled object
    Specified token addresses, coins or accounts
    Trigger
    A final, valid order that identifies its target with reasonable particularity
    Legal role
    The order may require seizing, freezing, burning or blocking transfer of coins.
    Action
    Check the order, identify the target and use ledger or account controls.
    Downstream effect
    An administrator's on-chain controls let a legal order reach the coins themselves.
    Customer view
    A targeted balance or account may become non-transferable.
    Merchant view
    A processor may reject the affected credential or asset.
    Failure mode
    Assuming the reserve custodian normally edits the token ledger.
    Proof needed
    Rules for who holds the keys, an authorization matrix, the procedure, an audit log and an appeal or review path.
    Boundary
    The statute requires a specific, reviewable lawful order, not an open-ended discretionary command.
    Sources: 12 U.S.C. §5901

    Which coins you can use in the U.S. will come down to each platform's listFuture implementation pattern

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Track
    Who may sell or offer the coin
    Evidence state
    Statute (enacted law)
    Entity
    Custodial wallet / exchange
    Status
    future implementation pattern
    Controller
    The wallet's or exchange's compliance and product team
    Controlled object
    Permission to buy, sell, hold, deposit, withdraw and transfer, by asset and region
    Trigger
    A change in issuer eligibility, a lawful-order designation or the 2028 deadline
    Legal role
    The provider may offer or sell to U.S. persons only the coins the statute allows.
    Action
    Change the asset's status and notify customers; keep or limit wind-down functions as required.
    Downstream effect
    The blockchain can keep running while U.S. commercial access shrinks.
    Customer view
    Buttons gray out, deposits stop, and sell-only or withdrawal-only modes may appear.
    Merchant view
    Payment from custodial wallets can disappear from checkout.
    Failure mode
    Confusing custody or account restrictions with destruction of the token.
    Proof needed
    Supported-asset table, region rules, notices and enforcement record.
    Boundary
    Exact wind-down behavior will depend on final rules and provider obligations.
    Sources: 12 U.S.C. §5901 · 12 U.S.C. §5902

    A seller like PayPal controls your account without owing you the dollarCustomer contract in force

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Track
    Who may sell or offer the coin
    Evidence state
    Contract in force
    Entity
    PayPal Digital
    Status
    customer contract in force
    Controller
    PayPal Digital
    Controlled object
    Customer access, pooled (omnibus) custody of tokens and supported crypto functions
    Trigger
    PayPal product policy, legal eligibility or account controls
    Legal role
    PayPal Digital is the crypto-services and custody counterparty that customers deal with; Paxos issues PYUSD.
    Action
    Turn customer functions on or off, while Paxos's issuer obligation stays in place.
    Downstream effect
    The power to sell the coin and the duty to redeem it are regulated separately.
    Customer view
    PayPal looks like the product, even though Paxos owes redemption.
    Merchant view
    PayPal checkout can look the same while the supported asset behind it changes.
    Failure mode
    Attributing Paxos's reserve and redemption duties to PayPal.
    Proof needed
    Current PayPal customer terms, current Paxos terms and supported-asset policy.
    Boundary
    Pooled (omnibus) custody of customer tokens is not custody of the reserves backing PYUSD.
    Sources: PayPal — cryptocurrency terms · Paxos — stablecoin terms

    Payment processors decide which coins merchants can accept; the terminal hardware barely mattersWorking thesis · watching for implementation

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Track
    Who may sell or offer the coin
    Evidence state
    Plan, not yet in effect
    Entity
    Processor / acquirer / network
    Status
    working thesis · watching for implementation
    Controller
    Product and risk systems at the processor, acquirer or network
    Controlled object
    Enabled payment methods, settlement assets, blockchains, regions and merchant eligibility
    Trigger
    An approved issuer and asset plus a commercial rollout, or a loss of eligibility
    Legal role
    The intermediary decides whether and how an eligible stablecoin is used for merchant acceptance or settlement.
    Action
    Add or remove a software payment method, wallet credential, settlement asset or merchant setting.
    Downstream effect
    Contactless terminals installed for tokenized wallets can take payments funded or settled through a new network.
    Customer view
    Customers tap or pick a familiar wallet; the asset behind it may change.
    Merchant view
    A dashboard or contract change can matter more than new hardware.
    Failure mode
    Claiming Apple Pay itself sends crypto, or that every NFC terminal can natively accept any token.
    Proof needed
    Named processor or network product, flow of funds, settlement asset, merchant terms and which terminals and software are supported.
    Boundary
    Apple Pay is a comparison and an example of a service linking wallets to terminals. It is not a stablecoin and not proof of crypto acceptance.
    Sources: Apple — Apple Pay security and tokenization · 12 U.S.C. §5902

    Self-custody wallets and software are not covered by the 2028 platform rule

    Deck as of: 2026-07-28
    Track
    Who may sell or offer the coin
    Evidence state
    Statute (enacted law)
    Entity
    Protocol / self-custody software
    Status
    exclusions written into the statute's definitions
    Controller
    The user and the protocol's rules, subject to other law
    Controlled object
    Private keys and use of the protocol
    Trigger
    A self-custody transaction the user directs
    Legal role
    The digital-asset service provider (DASP) definition excludes specified protocols, building or running a protocol, immutable self-custody software, validators and liquidity-pool participation.
    Action
    Protocol access continues even where custodial commercial sales shrink.
    Downstream effect
    What is legally available differs by software and custody model.
    Customer view
    A self-custodied token can still move after an exchange delists it.
    Merchant view
    Accepting coins directly on-chain stays technically possible, but runs separately from covered processors.
    Failure mode
    Turning a rule for intermediaries into a ban on holding or transferring coins.
    Proof needed
    Exact service design, how it is paid, facts on custody and control, and final interpretive guidance.
    Boundary
    The exclusion does not protect against sanctions, fraud, money-transmission or other applicable law.
    Sources: 12 U.S.C. §5901

    3 · The cash leg: what kind of money settles

    The cash-legs family and its walkthrough

    · 1 rows

    Read from: _Workspace/Indexes/masters/crypto_hub/families.json › cash-legs

    Cash legs & tokenized bank money

    Home checked through: 2026-09-01
    Kind
    money claims + settlement rails
    Description
    Compare stablecoins, commercial-bank deposit tokens, central-bank-backed settlement units and orchestration layers without treating them as interchangeable cash.
    Boundary
    Issuer liability, representation, orchestration and legal discharge remain separate.
    Walkthrough
    • Identify the money issuer: central bank, commercial bank or stablecoin issuer.
    • Identify the legal claim: reserve, deposit, redemption right or payment-system balance.
    • Identify its representation: conventional account, private ledger or public-chain token.
    • Identify eligible access: customer, vetted wallet, participant or settlement bank.
    • Identify the orchestration layer: application, Swift, Partior or smart contract.
    • Locate final settlement or redemption on bank books, RTGS, reserve pool or same ledger.
    • Preserve correction, insolvency, downtime and operating-clock boundaries.
    Root node
    Cash-leg architecture
    Nodes in this family
    • Cash-leg architecture
    • Commercial-bank money
    • Central-bank money

    Request for Comment on Innovative Methods To Detect Illicit Activity Involving Digital AssetsOfficial

    Date: 2025-08-18
    Date
    2025-08-18
    Agency
    Treasury Department
    Type
    Notice
    Summary
    The U.S. Department of the Treasury invites interested members of the public to provide input on the use of innovative or novel methods, techniques, or strategies to detect and mitigate illicit finance risks involving digital assets. This notice fulfills a requirement of the Guiding and Establishing National Innovation for U.S. Stablecoins Act (GENIUS Act) and supports the Administration's policy of supporting the responsible growth and use of digital assets, as outlined in the January 23, 2025, Executive Order 14178 on "Strengthening American Leadership in Digital Financial Technology."
    Tags
    stablecoin, genius act, digital asset
    Record kind
    official
    Sources: federalregister.gov

    GENIUS Act ImplementationOfficial

    Date: 2025-09-19
    Date
    2025-09-19
    Agency
    Treasury Department
    Type
    Proposed Rule
    Summary
    The Department of the Treasury (Treasury) is issuing this advance notice of proposed rulemaking (ANPRM) to solicit public comment on questions relating to the implementation of the Guiding and Establishing National Innovation for U.S. Stablecoins (GENIUS) Act. The GENIUS Act tasks Treasury (and various other federal agencies) with issuing regulations that encourage innovation in payment stablecoins while also providing an appropriately tailored regime to protect consumers, mitigate potential illicit finance risks, and address financial stability risks. Through this ANPRM, Treasury is seeking public comment on potential regulations that may be promulgated by Treasury, including regarding regulatory clarity, prohibitions on certain issuances and marketing, Bank Secrecy Act (BSA) anti-money laundering (AML) and sanctions obligations, the balance of state-level oversight with federal oversight, comparable foreign regulatory and supervisory regimes, and tax issues, among other things. Treasury is seeking comment on all aspects of the ANPRM from all interested parties and also requests commenters to identify other issues that Treasury should consider.
    Tags
    stablecoin, genius act, payment stablecoin
    Record kind
    official
    Sources: federalregister.gov

    Date: 2026-02-11
    Date
    2026-02-11
    Agency
    Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation
    Type
    Proposed Rule
    Summary
    On December 19, 2025, the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation (FDIC) published in the Federal Register a notice of proposed rulemaking that would establish procedures to be followed by an insured State nonmember bank or State savings association (each, an FDIC-supervised institution) that seeks to obtain FDIC approval to issue payment stablecoins through a subsidiary pursuant to the Guiding and Establishing National Innovation for U.S. Stablecoins Act (GENIUS Act). The proposed rule provided for a 60-day comment period, which will close on February 17, 2026. The FDIC has determined that an extension of the comment period until May 18, 2026, is appropriate. This action will allow interested parties additional time to analyze the proposal and prepare comments.

    Investments in and Licensing of Permitted Payment Stablecoins IssuersOfficial

    Date: 2026-02-12
    Date
    2026-02-12
    Agency
    National Credit Union Administration
    Type
    Proposed Rule
    Summary
    The NCUA Board (Board) is seeking comment on proposed regulations to implement portions of the Guiding and Establishing National Innovation for U.S. Stablecoins Act (GENIUS Act). The GENIUS Act charges the NCUA with licensing, regulating, and supervising payment stablecoin issuers that are subsidiaries of federally insured credit unions (FICU subsidiaries). The GENIUS Act also requires the NCUA to issue implementing regulations by July 18th, 2026. This proposed rule proposes regulations to implement the statutorily required process for approval and licensure of permitted payment stablecoin issuers (PPSIs) subject to the NCUA's jurisdiction. It also proposes regulations limiting FICUs to investing in NCUA-licensed PPSIs. A forthcoming proposal will propose regulations to implement the standards and restrictions imposed by the GENIUS Act on PPSIs.
    Tags
    stablecoin, genius act, payment stablecoin
    Record kind
    official
    Sources: federalregister.gov

    OCC's GENIUS Implementation NPRM — and the Yield-Loophole WarOfficial

    Date: 2026-03-02
    Date

    Permitted Payment Stablecoin Issuer Anti-Money Laundering/Countering the Financing of Terrorism Program and Sanctions Compliance Program RequirementsOfficial

    Date: 2026-04-10
    Date
    2026-04-10
    Agency
    Treasury Department — Foreign Assets Control Office
    Type
    Proposed Rule
    Summary
    The Department of the Treasury's Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) and Office of Foreign Assets Control (OFAC) are jointly issuing this proposed rule to implement provisions of the Guiding and Establishing National Innovation for U.S. Stablecoins Act (GENIUS Act). Specifically, it implements the GENIUS Act's directive to treat permitted payment stablecoin issuers (PPSIs) as financial institutions for purposes of the Bank Secrecy Act, proposes anti-money laundering obligations for PPSIs, and proposes certain specific obligations required by the GENIUS Act for PPSIs. It also implements the GENIUS Act's directive to require PPSIs to maintain effective sanctions compliance programs.
    Tags
    stablecoin, genius act, payment stablecoin
    Record kind
    official
    Sources: federalregister.gov

    FinCEN Breaks Three Weeks of Silence — Gacki Before the HouseOfficial

    Date: 2026-07-21
    Date
    2026-07-21
    Agency
    FinCEN
    Type
    Report
    Summary
    Director Andrea Gacki testifies to the House Financial Services Subcommittee on National Security, Illicit Finance and International Financial Institutions. On GENIUS: FinCEN 'has also issued two proposed rules to implement AML and customer identification program requirements for permitted payment stablecoin issuers... designed to be fit for purpose... and minimize unnecessary burden,' and 'the necessary resources for FinCEN to fulfill additional supervisory responsibilities, fully implement the GENIUS Act... are included in FinCEN's FY 2027 Budget Request.'     The Adapter Constitution Lands — Senate CLARITY Joins the Private Rails to the Control PlaneOfficial

    Date: 2026-07-22
    Date
    2026-07-22
    Agency
    U.S. Senate — Banking and Agriculture negotiating product
    Type
    Public Substitute Draft — Not Law
    Summary
    The 616-page EHF26654 substitute publicly joins the Senate Banking and Agriculture market-structure products: SEC/CFTC asset and intermediary perimeters, bank authority, qualified custody, stablecoin-reward limits, software and self-custody shields, portfolio margin, a Treasury Digital Asset Cyber Innovation Center, state enforcement grants, and GENIUS lawful-order procedures for freezing, burning and reissuing stablecoins. The permanent structure legalizes private programmable rails while specifying their supervision and interruption points. Its amendment number, proposer and committee fields remain blank: public draft, not formally filed amendment, Senate-passed bill, law or running effective-date clock.

    Sanctioning a Gold Token — OFAC Names Tala and the Dot One RailsOfficial

    Date: 2026-07-24
    Date
    2026-07-24
    Agency
    OFAC
    Type
    Regulatory Action
    Summary
    Four individuals and nine entities designated under E.O. 13902 (Treasury release sb0576). The new thing: OFAC names DotOne Gold Company (a.k.a. TALA GOLD / TALA TOKEN) as gold-bar minter and custodian 'integral to the operations of Tala Token, an allegedly gold-backed digital asset token,' with significant quantities passing through Zedcex infrastructure in early 2025. Istanbul's Zedpay is cut off as the fiat on-ramp 'directly integrated into Zedxion's platform'; Dubai's Zedx DMCC handled Zedcex exchange wallets; DotOne Barter is described as the conglomerate's clearing house. Follows the Jan 30, 2026 designation of Zanjani and Zedcex. Note what is absent: no digital-currency-address fields in the entries, so no automatic issuer freeze fired — unlike Jul 14, when Tether froze about $131M within the hour.

    What is uncertain

    • Primary sources only (Katie, 2026-10-19): official records and the actor's own documents. News, newsletters, blogs and law-firm summaries were removed on 2026-10-19; what rested on them is held in masters/_held/needs_primary_source_2026-10-19.json.
    • Market sizes are issuer-published figures only (the issuer's own page or the attestation report it publishes), each with its own date and basis. DefiLlama and other aggregators are not used (Katie's primary-source rule, 2026-10-19).
    • genius_route is the path the issuer is on or most likely to use; it is never a finding that approval was granted. No GENIUS approvals exist yet because the implementing rules are not final.
    • The July decks are dated July 28–29, 2026 and are history; where they disagree with the coin rows above, the coin rows (checked October 19, 2026) are current.
    • World rows may keep news links (the international room's exception); each says its kind of record.
    • No machine-readable conflict file exists for stablecoins.json in this workbench; recorded world conflicts are printed on their rows with every reading.

    Sources and data

    • Master: stablecoins.json (coins, watchlist). Packages read in place: the five July card decks under Retail Payment Terminal Transition 2026-07-28 (the law deck's home is stablecoin_laws.json, on link A).
    • Crypto Hub: masters/crypto_hub/families.json (cash-legs) and Research Packages/Fintech and Private Rails/Tokenization Entity and Control Map 2026-09-01/TOKENIZATION_ENTITY_RELATIONSHIP_REGISTRY_2026-09-01.json (nodes, edges, the cash-leg lens).
    • Dated records: masters/records/money.json (stablecoins track), masters/records/world.json (ledger track) with masters/conflicts/world_disclosure_CONFLICTS.json.
    • Download the data: link-b-money.json.
